# Contract changes

This file lists every behaviour change made against the goldens recorded at
`8361f23b40c3daf08a64ed4f4c43f518807468e4`, the commit `refactor/v1` was cut
from. The goldens pin current behaviour; a refactor item that changes
behaviour on purpose records the change here, one entry per change, and lands
the entry in the same pull request as the change it describes. An entry names
the route (or seam) touched, the behaviour before, the behaviour after, and
the item that made the change. Refactors that keep behaviour identical do not
get an entry.

| Item | Route / seam | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| CP-S03 (CP-04, CP-08) | staff document (`GET`, `POST`, `PUT` of `/api/v1/admin/staff*`) | The staff answer carried no PIN facts | Every staff answer carries `counter_pin_set` and `locked`, the two facts the staff screen shows of the manager's counter PIN (migration 137); the argon2id hash never leaves the server. The goldens for `staff` and `staff_detail` were regenerated for the two added fields |
| CP-S03 (CP-04, CP-08) | `PUT`, `DELETE /api/v1/admin/staff/{id}/counter-pin`, `POST /api/v1/admin/staff/{id}/counter-pin/unlock` | No such routes: a manager's counter PIN could not be set, cleared or unlocked anywhere | New admin and owner routes. The set takes a 4 to 8 digit PIN (never one repeated digit or a plain run like 1234), hashes it with argon2id and a per PIN salt and clears the failures and the lock; the answer of all three is only `{counter_pin_set, locked}`; each act writes its audit row (`staff.counter_pin_set`, `_cleared`, `_unlocked`) without the PIN |
| CP-S03 (CP-04, CP-08) | `POST /api/v1/pos/step-up`, `GET /api/v1/pos/step-up/managers` | No such routes: a manager's approval at the counter existed only as the signed in caller's own claims, so a cashier could never get one | The step up: any person at the terminal (never a machine key) asks with the manager (staff number or id), the PIN, the act (`override`, `pickup`, `signoff`) and its target (plus the amount for a pickup). The server verifies the PIN in constant time, counts failures (five in a row lock the PIN until an admin unlocks it; a wrong manager, an inactive one, a missing PIN and a wrong PIN all answer the same 403 `step_up_refused` "That PIN is not right", a locked one 403 `step_up_locked`), checks the act's approving roles and the act's own person rules (403 `step_up_own_sale`, `step_up_own_drawer`), and mints one single use approval `{token, manager_name, expires_at}` stored only as its SHA-256 hash (table `pos_step_up_approvals`, migration 137), expiring minutes ahead. The managers list answers the roster the picker shows for the act |
| CP-S03 (CP-04, CP-08) | `POST /api/v1/pos/transactions/{id}/overrides`, `POST /api/v1/pos/till/{id}/movements` (a pickup), `POST /api/v1/pos/till/{id}/signoff` | The approver, the pickup taker and the signer came only from the caller's own claims (or `dev-mode`); a pickup's taker needed the two call confirm flow | Each act takes an `approval_token`: when present the act spends the step up approval inside its own transaction (one use, the right act, target and amount, unexpired; anything else 409 `step_up_spent`) and records the manager it names by name in the existing columns (`approved_by`, `taken_by`, `signoff_by`) and the audit row. The claims paths stand as they were, and under `AUTH_MODE=dev` a call with neither claims nor a token keeps the `dev-mode` answers. A pickup with a token needs no `taken_by` (the manager is named by the approval, never typed) and needs no confirm call |
| CP-S03 (CP-08) | `GET /api/v1/pos/till/signoffs`, `POST /api/v1/pos/till/{id}/signoff` (mount) | Both sat behind the finance and owner resolve mount, so a cashier could neither see the waiting drawers nor reach the act | The queue and the act sit behind the counter's own guard (admin, owner, cashier): a cashier sees the queue and can spend a manager's step up approval at the terminal. The claims path of the act keeps the finance and owner rule in the handler itself; only a token walks a cashier through |
| R1-4 | idempotency retention purge (operational, not a wire change) | the nightly purge of expired `idempotency_keys` rows ran inside the server process, started at server boot | the purge runs in the worker role of the one core binary (`core worker`) only; `core serve` and the old `cmd/server` no longer start it, so a deployment that runs no worker accumulates expired `idempotency_keys` rows until one runs |
| R1-11 | POST, PUT and PATCH routes carrying an idempotency key (the server-wide middleware) | Only `X-Idempotency-Key` was read; the stored response lived in an in-process map, so a replay worked only inside one process and a retry after a restart re-ran the handler and repeated the write | `Idempotency-Key` is the canonical header and `X-Idempotency-Key` stays an alias addressing the same claim; claims and stored responses live in the `idempotency_keys` table (migration 087), so a replay after a restart or on another instance returns the stored response, and a replay carries `Idempotency-Replayed: true` |
| R1-11 | POST, PUT and PATCH routes carrying an idempotency key | A second request with the same key while the first was in flight re-ran the handler (nothing coordinated concurrent duplicates), and a key reused with a different request silently re-ran too | A concurrent duplicate gets `409` with the machine code `idempotency_in_progress` while the first is in progress, and a key reused with a different request gets `422` with the machine code `idempotency_key_reused`; both answers use the standard error envelope (code, message, an empty `details` array, the request id in `meta`) |
| R1-11 | POST, PUT and PATCH routes carrying an idempotency key | A `4xx` or `5xx` outcome was never at stake (the in-memory cache stored nothing) | Only `2xx` and `3xx` outcomes are stored and replayed; a `4xx` or `5xx` releases the claim so the client can retry with the same key |
| R1-11 | POST, PUT and PATCH routes carrying an idempotency key | Keys were effectively global: the middleware ran before auth, so all callers shared one anonymous namespace and one caller's stored response could be replayed to another caller who reused the key | Keys are scoped to the caller, one layer per surface: the global middleware (inside JWT auth) covers the ERP API keyed on the JWT subject and skips `/api/portal/v1/` and `/api/integration/`; portal routes carry the layer inside the portal auth chain, keyed on the portal customer and user; integration routes carry it inside the `X-Integration-Key` check, keyed on the caller's `X-Tenant-ID` tenant. Under `AUTH_MODE=dev` a caller with no identity is the fixed `dev` principal, and an integration caller without a tenant gets its own `dev:integration` principal so it never shares the global layer's dev namespace; outside dev mode such a caller passes through uncached |
| R1-11 | POST, PUT and PATCH routes with query flags carrying an idempotency key | The query string was not part of the request fingerprint, so `?dry_run=true` and `?dry_run=false` under one key replayed the first response | The query string, canonicalized by key then value, is part of the fingerprint: a key reused with a different query string gets `422` `idempotency_key_reused`, while reordered spellings of the same query still replay |
| R1-11 | POST, PUT and PATCH routes carrying an idempotency key whose body exceeds the 10 MB request size limit | The middleware never read the body; the handler's read failed against the limit and the handler answered | The middleware reads the body for the fingerprint inside that same limit and answers `413` with the error envelope (code `payload_too_large`) itself, before any claim is made; any other body read failure answers `400` `bad_request` |
| R1-11 | POST, PUT and PATCH routes carrying an `Idempotency-Key` outside 1 to 255 printable ASCII characters | The key was accepted as given (an overlong key travelled to the database and the request was then served uncached through the fail-open path) | `400` `validation_failed` naming the header in the message and in `details`, before any claim is made |
| R1-11 | POST, PUT and PATCH routes carrying an idempotency key whose response is a redirect or carries a `Location` header | Nothing was stored for a `3xx` (the claim was released and a retry re-ran the handler), and a stored `201`'s `Location` was dropped on replay | `2xx` and `3xx` outcomes are stored with status, `Content-Type`, `Location` and body, and replayed with all of them; `Set-Cookie` is never stored, so never replayed. A stored body is capped at 1 MiB: a larger outcome is served to its caller in full, the claim is released and the drop logged, so a retry re-runs the handler |
| R1-11 | POST, PUT and PATCH routes carrying an idempotency key while the database is unreachable | No such case existed (the cache was in memory) | The request is served uncached (fail open); nothing is claimed or replayed and a warning is logged |
| R1-13 | `Authorization: Bearer sk_live_*` on `/api/v1` module routes | No machine key authentication existed: the auth middleware JWT-parsed every Bearer token, so a machine key always failed with 401 | A machine key (the `sk_live_` shape `GenerateKey` mints) is validated against `api_keys` and needs the route module's scope, `<module>:read` for GET and HEAD, `<module>:write` for every other method, the module being the first path segment under `/api/v1/`. A key without the scope gets 403 `forbidden` and the refusal writes an `audit_log` row attributed to the key (kind `key`, the key's id in `actor_id`, the refused scope in `changes`). An unknown or revoked key gets 401 `unauthorized`; the key store being down gets 503 `unavailable`. Key management under `/api/v1/admin/keys` is user-only: a key is refused 403 whatever scope it holds. In `AUTH_MODE=dev` the same rules apply to a machine key Bearer (other dev callers keep the dev bypass). A machine key's idempotency claims key on `key:<id>` in every auth mode (the R1-11 ERP layer), so a keyed caller's retries are cached instead of passing uncached or joining the dev namespace. `/api/integration/*` is untouched: it stays `X-Integration-Key` only, and a machine-key-only request there is refused by the seam's own missing-key 401. See `docs/adr/0002-machine-keys.md` |
| R1-13 | Auth middleware error responses on non-public paths (401 no or invalid token, 403 insufficient role, from the JWT middleware and `RequireRole`) | `{"error":{"code":"UNAUTHORIZED","message":"Unauthorized"}}` style bodies: uppercase codes and a generic status text, the specific cause only in the server log | The wire ADR's error envelope: lowercase snake_case codes (`unauthorized`, `forbidden`), the specific message kept verbatim (for example "no token provided", "invalid token", "insufficient role"), and `meta.request_id`. The JWT validation semantics are unchanged; only the error bodies convert, which is the middleware conversion ADR 0001 assigns to this item |
| R1-13 | POS routes that resolve their cashier from the request identity (`POST /api/v1/pos/transactions`, `POST /api/v1/pos/till/open`, `POST /api/v1/pos/returns`) | The cashier came from the JWT subject, or in dev mode from a fabricated stand-in UUID when no identity was present, for every caller alike | A machine key is refused 403 `forbidden` with the message "a cashier must be a user" (the wire ADR's envelope): a key is not a user and never receives the fabricated cashier. Keyless dev callers keep the fallback. See `docs/adr/0002-machine-keys.md` |
| R1-14 | `audit_log` table / every audited mutation (order confirm, cancel, fulfil; payment process, refund; invoice create; deposit record, apply; POS complete, void, return) | Audit rows were written by a goroutine through the pool after the mutation, so a rolled back mutation could still leave an audit row and a crash could lose one | Audit rows are written synchronously inside the mutation's transaction: a rolled back mutation leaves no row, a committed one leaves exactly one, and a failed audit write fails the mutation. Rows also carry the actor's kind (`user`, `key`, `agent`, `anonymous`), the actor's id (always the resolved actor; `user_id` keeps the legacy explicit attribution, and stays NULL when a machine key made the write with no explicit attribution, so key ids appear only in `actor_id`; an empty attribution of any kind is stored as NULL, the value the 088 backfill already writes for such rows), and for an agent the `X-Acting-As` marker and `X-Agent-Tool` tool name. The table is indexed on `(actor_kind, actor_id)` |
| R1-14 | `pkg/audit` `Log` seam | Fire-and-forget, no error return | Synchronous; returns an error (callers inside a transaction propagate it, callers without one log it and continue). With no transaction in the context the write runs under `context.WithoutCancel` plus a 5s timeout, so a client disconnect after the commit no longer drops the row for a committed mutation |
| R1-14 | Requests carrying `X-Acting-As` and `X-Agent-Tool` | Headers ignored; agent mutations were indistinguishable from the user's own | The marker (recorded lowercased) and tool name are recorded on the audit row. Each value is capped at 128 bytes of printable ASCII; a longer or non-printable value is refused with a 400 `validation_failed` naming the header, in the wire ADR's error envelope shape. The marker never grants anything; it only records |
| R1-14 | CORS preflight (`Access-Control-Allow-Headers`) | `X-Acting-As` and `X-Agent-Tool` not allowed, so a cross-origin agentic UI failed preflight | Both headers join the allowed list |
| R1-14 | Audit rows with no identity (dev mode, background jobs) | Labelled `kind='user'` with an empty actor id | Labelled `kind='anonymous'` with a null actor id; the 088 backfill labels userless rows (null or empty `user_id`) the same way |
| R1-14 | POS `POST /api/v1/pos/transactions/{id}/void` (audit side only) | Voiding an already-voided transaction wrote a second `pos.transaction.voided` audit row | Only a void that actually changes state writes an audit row |
| R1-14 | card payments (`ProcessCardPayment`, the gateway-charged path) | Card payments wrote no audit row at all, so card money movement was invisible to the financial audit trail while cash payments were audited | A committed card payment writes one `payment.processed` audit row inside the transaction that records the payment (the gateway charge still runs outside it, before it), so the row shares the payment's fate |
| R1-12 | `GET /api/v1/events` | No such route: outside consumers had no event feed and polled domain tables | New route, the events read API over the `events_outbox` table (migration 089). Returns the ADR 0001 list envelope `{items, next_cursor, limit}` (`total` only under `?include=total`) of events in commit order, each item the snake_case event envelope `{event_id, type, org, branch_id, entity {kind, id}, data, at}` with `at` an RFC 3339 UTC timestamp at microsecond precision (the refactor inputs' envelope is camelCase `eventId, branchId`; ADR 0001 section 12 adopts snake_case on purpose, and this row is the record of that choice on the feed that first ships it). `next_cursor` is always present, an explicit exception to ADR 0001's null-at-end rule recorded in ADR 0003 section 5: the feed is a poller's tail, so the cursor is the last served position, or the request's cursor echoed back when the page is empty, and a poller never has to re-read to keep its position. Query parameters are exactly `cursor`, `limit`, `types` and `include`: a malformed cursor, an unknown parameter, a bad limit, an unknown include name or a malformed type name is a 400 naming the field, and the error is the standard envelope. Role gated `admin, owner`, the admin reads' narrowest gate. The route's contract fragment (`core/api/fragments/events.yaml`) landed in R1-12b together with the lowercase error envelope in `_shared.yaml` (`WireError`); the route left `core/api/contract-pending.txt`, and a golden group (`events`) pins the feed's pages, its strict query errors and its always present `next_cursor`. The 401 and 403 of the route stay the legacy envelope until R1-11 converts the auth middleware and role guard |
| R1-12 | the lumber price exposure notification seam (`pkg/eventbus` publishers in the exposure scanner and service) | The scanner and service published notification events straight onto the in-process bus after their writes: best-effort, at-most-once, lost on a restart, invisible outside the process | Both record their notification events into the `events_outbox` table inside the mutation's transaction (a rolled back mutation leaves no event; a failed event write fails the mutation), and a drain runner delivers the committed rows synchronously to the notifier's handler, one row at a time, with the outbox `event_id` as the event id, so the exposure emails still send, now following the commit rather than the process lifetime. Delivery through the drain is at-least-once (the cursor advances past a row only when its handler returned nil; a failing row is retried and, after 10 attempts, parked in `event_subscriber_parked` while the cursor moves on); the notifier dedups on the outbox event id in process, and that dedup is deliberately not durable in v1, so a restart during a replay can send a duplicate email (ADR 0003 section 4 records the acceptance) |
| R1-7ct | quote: POST /api/v1/quotes | The success body is JSON the handler encodes without a Content-Type header, so Go sniffs it as `text/plain; charset=utf-8` | `Content-Type: application/json` set before the body write; status codes and body bytes unchanged. Golden `quote` re-recorded (7 steps) |
| R1-7ct | order: POST /api/v1/orders | Same missing Content-Type on the 201 body | `Content-Type: application/json` set before the body write; status codes and body bytes unchanged. Golden `order` re-recorded (2 steps); its `<id-3>` and `<id-4>` placeholders swap labels because the harness numbers placeholders by first appearance and the unwrapped body is now walked in sorted key order, a recording artefact, not a value change |
| R1-7ct | customer: POST /api/v1/customers; POST /api/v1/customers/{customerId}/contacts | Same missing Content-Type on the 201 body | `Content-Type: application/json` set before the body write; status codes and body bytes unchanged. Golden `customer` re-recorded (the contacts create has no golden yet, R1-1b) |
| R1-7ct | crm: POST /api/v1/customers/{customerId}/activities | Same missing Content-Type on the 201 body | `Content-Type: application/json` set before the body write; status codes and body bytes unchanged. Golden `crm` re-recorded |
| R1-7ct | product: POST /api/v1/products | Same missing Content-Type on the 201 body | `Content-Type: application/json` set before the body write; status codes and body bytes unchanged. Golden `product` re-recorded |
| R1-7ct | vendor: POST /api/v1/vendors; GET /api/v1/vendors/{id} | Same missing Content-Type on the 201 and 200 bodies | `Content-Type: application/json` set before the body write; status codes and body bytes unchanged. Golden `vendor` re-recorded |
| R1-7ct | pricing: POST /api/v1/pricing/rules | Same missing Content-Type on the 201 body | `Content-Type: application/json` set before the body write; status codes and body bytes unchanged. Golden `pricing` re-recorded |
| R1-7ct | millwork: POST /api/v1/millwork/options | Same missing Content-Type on the 201 body | `Content-Type: application/json` set before the body write; status codes and body bytes unchanged. Golden `millwork` re-recorded |
| R1-7ct | delivery: POST /api/v1/delivery/vehicles; POST /api/v1/delivery/drivers; POST /api/v1/delivery/routes; POST /api/v1/delivery/routes/{id}/complete; POST /api/v1/delivery/deliveries/{id}/adjust-qty | Same missing Content-Type on the JSON bodies | `Content-Type: application/json` set before the body write; status codes and body bytes unchanged. Golden `delivery` re-recorded (vehicles and drivers; the other three routes have no golden yet, R1-1b) |
| R1-7ct | purchase_order: POST /api/v1/purchase-orders/{id}/submit; POST /api/v1/purchase-orders/{id}/receive; POST /api/v1/purchase-orders/reorder-check | Same missing Content-Type on the 200 bodies | `Content-Type: application/json` set before the body write; status codes and body bytes unchanged. No golden yet for these routes (R1-1b) |
| R1-7ct | document: POST /api/v1/invoices/{id}/email | Same missing Content-Type on the 202 body (a literal JSON string written with `w.Write`) | `Content-Type: application/json` set before the body write; status codes and body bytes unchanged. No golden yet for this route (R1-1b) |
| R1-7c | portal: POST /api/portal/v1/quotes; POST /api/portal/v1/orders/reorder; POST /api/portal/v1/deliveries/{id}/reschedule; POST /api/portal/v1/cart/items; POST /api/portal/v1/checkout; POST /api/portal/v1/invites | The handlers called WriteHeader before the JSON helper set the Content-Type header, so the header never reached the wire and Go sniffed the JSON body as `text/plain; charset=utf-8` | The helper sets `Content-Type: application/json` and then writes the status (`portalWriteJSONStatus`), so no caller can write the status first; status codes and body bytes unchanged. Golden `portal` re-recorded (cart add); the other five routes have no golden yet (R1-1b) |
| R1-7c | project: POST /api/portal/v1/projects | Same missing Content-Type on the 201 body | `writeJSONStatus` sets the header before the status; status and body bytes unchanged. Golden `project` re-recorded (create; in `project.get` the `<id-1>` and `<id-2>` placeholders swap, a recording artefact of first appearance numbering, not a value change) |
| R1-12 | quote exposure: POST /api/v1/quotes/{id}/exposure/escalate-now (and every service path that lists a quote's active escalators: the exposure scanner's rollup inside each real scan transaction, which hit the same error on a real database and rolled the scan back, and the acknowledge and override escalator flips) | The escalator listing query selected unqualified columns across a join with `quote_lines`, which Postgres rejects as ambiguous (`id`), so the route answered 500 `INTERNAL_ERROR` for any quote with an escalator; the recorded golden pinned that 500 | The query selects from `price_escalators` alone, filtered by a `quote_lines` subquery, and the route answers 200 with the escalation preview (`quote_id`, `estimated_new_total`, `lines`). The fix is required by the acknowledgment transaction, which lists escalators inside it. Golden `exposure` re-recorded (one step) |
| R1-12b | the events outbox table `events_outbox` (operational, no route or wire shape changes) | Rows were never deleted: nothing bounded the table | The worker role purges rows older than `OUTBOX_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 14, zero turns it off), hourly, in batches of 500 with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`. A row is kept while any registered subscriber cursor has not reached it, and while an `event_subscriber_parked` entry names it (until an operator deletes the entry); with no subscriber registered age alone decides. A feed consumer further behind than the age resumes from the oldest retained event (ADR 0003 section 6) |
| R1-sec | rate limiting client address (operational, not a wire change): the global limit (120 per minute) and the portal login limit (10 per minute), and the `remote_addr` field of the request log | The client was the leftmost `X-Forwarded-For` value whoever sent it, so a direct caller got a fresh budget per request by varying the header | The client is the TCP peer. `X-Forwarded-For` is read only when the peer is inside a network listed in the new `TRUSTED_PROXIES` setting (comma separated CIDRs, empty by default, a malformed entry stops boot); it is then walked from the right, skipping trusted hops, and the first untrusted address is the client. A deployment behind a load balancer must set `TRUSTED_PROXIES`, or every caller shares the balancer's budget. `X-Real-IP` is never read |
| R1-15 | quote: `GET /api/v1/quotes` | Offset page `{data, total, limit, offset}`; `total` on every page; `limit` and `offset` silently clamped to 1 to 200 and 0; any other parameter, `?status=sent` included, silently ignored (the inputs' live failure: sent filter returned drafts); items carried the bare legacy quote with float dollars and uppercase `state` | The list envelope `{items, next_cursor, limit}` newest first (`created_at`, then `id`, descending), `items` never null, `next_cursor` null on the last page, `total` only under `?include=total`. `cursor` is the opaque keyset cursor scoped `quotes.created_at_id_desc` (malformed or foreign: 400 `bad_request` naming `cursor`); `limit` is 1 to 200 and a bad value is a 400 `validation_failed` naming `limit`, never clamped; `offset` is gone (400 `unsupported_query_parameter`). The filters are `status` (comma separated lowercase list, an unknown value is a 400 naming `status`) and `customer_id`; any other parameter is a 400 `unsupported_query_parameter` naming it. Items are the quote header (`QuoteSummary`: no lines, no upload metadata, no exposure rollup) |
| R1-15 | quote: every quote body (`GET`/`POST`/`PUT /api/v1/quotes[/{id}]`, the transitions route, `GET /api/partner/v1/quotes[/{id}]`) | Lifecycle field `state` with UPPERCASE values; money as float dollars (`total_amount`, `freight_amount`, `margin_total`, `exposure_dollars`; line `unit_price`, `unit_cost`, `line_total`); `quantity` a JSON number; `delivery_type` and `exposure_state` UPPERCASE; optional fields omitted when empty; timestamps in the database zone's RFC 3339 with variable fraction; no document number; no revision | `status` (lowercase `draft`, `sent`, `accepted`, `rejected`, `expired`); `total_cents`, `freight_cents`, `margin_total_cents`, `exposure_cents` as int64 cents; lines carry `quantity` as a decimal string, `uom`, `price_uom`, the pair `uom_qty` and `price_uom_qty` (decimal strings, `"1"` and `"1"` when the units agree), `unit_price_ten_thousandths` (int64, scale 4), `line_total_cents` (the extension, rounded once, half away from zero) and `unit_cost_ten_thousandths` (read only, the product's average cost); `delivery_type` is `pickup` or `delivery`; `exposure_state` is lowercase on the quote wire (`ok`, `flagged`, `ack_required` and so on; the exposure routes keep their own UPPERCASE vocabulary until the pricing module converts); optional fields are present as `null` (`job_id`, `vehicle_id`, `vehicle_name`, the four lifecycle timestamps, `expires_at`, `original_filename`, `original_content_type`, `parse_map`, `exposure_last_checked_at`, a line's `product_id` and `customer_note`); every timestamp is RFC 3339 UTC with the Z at microsecond precision; new `number` (`Q-000123`, from the `quote_number_seq` sequence, backfilled in `(created_at, id)` order by migration 090, unique) and `revision` (starts at 1, moves on every write). Every read and write returns the revision as the `ETag` header (`"3"`) |
| R1-15 | quote: `POST /api/v1/quotes` request and behaviour | Decoded the full legacy quote shape with unknown fields ignored (a client could create a quote directly in any `state`, and send `total_amount`, `line_total`, `unit_cost`, which the server then recomputed or ignored); validation was loose: a line without `uom` failed at the database enum cast and answered 500 (the inputs' live failure); a bad `product_id` or `customer_id` was a 500; the success body carried no `Location`; no event was written | Request fields are `branch_id`, `customer_id` (required), `job_id`, `expires_at`, `delivery_type` (`pickup` default), `freight_cents`, `vehicle_id`, `source` (`manual` or `ai`), `margin_total_cents`, `original_file` (base64, 5 MB), `original_filename`, `original_content_type`, `parse_map`, `lines`; a field the route does not declare is a 400 `bad_request` naming it, and a created quote is always `draft` (the request has no status). Each line carries `quantity` (decimal string, not zero), `uom` (defaults from the product's own unit when the line names a product, and `price_uom` then defaults from it; a line with neither `uom` nor `product_id`, or an unknown unit, is a 400 `validation_failed` naming `lines[i].uom`), `unit_price_ten_thousandths` (integer, never negative), optionally `price_uom` (defaults to `uom`; when it differs, `uom_qty` and `price_uom_qty` are both required, positive), `sku` and `description` (default from `product_id`, required without one), `customer_note`, `id`. Every field problem of the request is collected into one 400 with a `details` entry per field (`lines[1].quantity`); an unknown product or customer is a 400 naming the field, no longer a 500. The total is the sum of the lines' extensions plus freight, freight cleared on a pickup. A `revision` in the create body is a 400 naming `revision` (a new quote has none; it used to be accepted and ignored). `price_uom` is a unit code of one to six capital letters (`^[A-Z]{1,6}$`: a price per `M` or `CWT` is allowed, free text is not). The response is 201 with `Location: /api/v1/quotes/{id}` and the `ETag`. The create writes `quote.created` (small summary: `number`, `customer_id`, `status`, `revision`, `total_cents`) into `events_outbox` as the last statement of its transaction, so a failed event write fails the create |

| R1-15 | quote: `PUT /api/v1/quotes/{id}` | Full replace with no concurrency check (two editors silently overwrote each other); a non draft quote answered 400 "only DRAFT quotes can be edited"; every other failure 500; the replacement keys and floats were the legacy shape | The write needs the client's revision, as `If-Match` (`"3"` or the weak `W/"3"`) or a `revision` field in the body: neither is 428 `precondition_required`, a stale one 409 `stale_revision`, `*` or a list of tags 400, and `If-Match` disagreeing with the body 400. The check and the write are one transaction (the row is locked first). A quote that is not a draft is 409 `conflict` ("only draft quotes can be edited") with a blocker `quote_not_draft`. The body is the create's header fields and lines: `branch_id`, `source`, `margin_total_cents`, `original_file`, `original_filename`, `original_content_type` and `parse_map` are fixed at create and sending one is a 400 `validation_failed` naming it (the base silently dropped them); a line whose `id` survives keeps its customer note and its place (lines keep their submitted order; they were unordered). An unknown `to` is a 400 `validation_failed` naming `to`; a transition the lifecycle does not allow (accepted is terminal; rejected and expired can only reopen to draft) is 409 `invalid_state_transition` ("cannot transition from accepted to sent"); no revision is 428, a stale one 409 `stale_revision`, an unknown quote 404. Each transition writes its event as the last statement of its transaction: `quote.sent`, `quote.accepted`, `quote.rejected`, `quote.expired`, or `quote.reopened` (to draft), with `number`, `customer_id`, `status`, `from_status`, `revision`, `total_cents`; a failed event write rolls the transition back |

The payload is built inside the transition's transaction before the status changes, so a refusal leaves the quote, its revision and its events as they were. A quote the lifecycle cannot accept (rejected, expired, already accepted) is 409 `invalid_state_transition`. Writes `quote.accepted`. Every field problem is collected into one 400 `validation_failed` with a `details` entry per field. The row is locked first and the check and the write are one transaction. `payment_terms_id` and `po_required` are required: a PUT that leaves either out is a 400 naming it, so a replace cannot silently reset a customer's terms or its PO requirement. Any other field left out takes its default (null for an optional one, `retail`, true). `primary_branch_id` is fixed at create and sending it is a 400. Changing the stored `currency` is 409 `conflict` with the blocker `open_documents` while the customer has an order not fulfilled or cancelled, an invoice or credit memo with an open amount, or a deposit with an unapplied amount; payment terms that are inactive cannot be newly assigned (a customer that already holds them keeps them). A field the route does not declare is a 400 (`customer_id` and `id` included: the path names the customer). Every field problem is collected into one 400; an unknown contact is 404 `not_found` on GET, PUT and DELETE. PUT replaces the contact on the client's revision (428, 409); `can_place_orders` and the `order_limit_cents` key are required (a 400 naming each when absent; an explicit `null` is still no limit of the contact's own), so a replace cannot re-grant order authority; any other field left out takes its default; DELETE needs the revision in `If-Match` (a DELETE has no body) and answers 204 with no body. List: the envelope newest first, `is_active` filters. PUT on the client's revision (428, 409), a field left out takes its default. The branch wall applies through the customer. | C2-1 | customer: new `GET`/`POST /api/v1/payment-terms`, `GET`/`PUT /api/v1/payment-terms/{id}` | No such routes; payment terms were free text on customers (`NET30` by default) and invoices | The payment terms master (ADR 0005 section 7.2): `code` (unique, capital letters, digits, underscore or hyphen, fixed once created), `name`, `kind` (`net_days`, `day_of_month`, `due_on_receipt`, lowercase), `net_days` (net_days terms), `day_of_month` (1 to 31; due on that day of the month after the invoice month, clamped to the month's length), `discount_percent` (a decimal string above 0 and at most 100) and `discount_days` (both or neither), `is_active`, `revision` and the `ETag`. Each kind's fields are validated together in one 400. Deactivate through `is_active`; there is no DELETE route (a term in use is never deleted), inactive terms cannot be newly assigned to a customer. List: the envelope newest first, `is_active` and `kind` filter. The migration seeds `NET30`, `NET60`, `NET90`, `DUE_ON_RECEIPT` and `COD` and maps the legacy text on customers and invoices without changing what it meant: matched case blind with spaces, dots, hyphens and underscores ignored, `NET <n>` (also `N<n>` and `NET <n> days`) is a NET n term (made when absent, code `NET<n>`, name `Net <n>`), `COD` and `cash on delivery` are COD, the due on receipt spellings are `DUE_ON_RECEIPT`; text that matches nothing gets a term of its own (the code the text normalised to the API's pattern with a numeric suffix when two normalise alike, the name the original text) and is reported in a `RAISE NOTICE`, never the NET30 default. The `NET_DAYS` 30 such a term carries is a guess (the text had no parseable meaning): an operator should read the notice list after migrating and correct each of those terms through `PUT /api/v1/payment-terms/{id}`. Reads are open to the roles that read customers (admin, owner, sales); creating and editing terms is restricted to admin, owner and finance. Writes no event (reference data; ADR 0005 section 12 names none). The invoice module takes its terms by id in C2-3, which drops the text columns |
| C2-1 | customer: machine key scope vocabulary (ADR 0002) | `customers`, `contacts` and `price_levels` were module segments; `ship-tos` and `payment-terms` did not exist | `ship-tos` and `payment-terms` join the vocabulary, so a key holding `ship-tos:read` or `payment-terms:write` reaches them and any other key is a 403 `forbidden` with an `audit_log` row |
| C2-1 | customer: credit limit semantics (the order credit gate, the account summary, the portal dashboard, the partner dashboard) | `customers.credit_limit` `0` (the column default) meant no limit; the order credit gate skipped a limit of `0` or less | `customers.credit_limit` is `NULL` for no limit and `0` is a limit of nothing; migration 091 turns every stored `0` into `NULL` and drops the column default, so no existing customer changes behaviour. The order credit gate treats a nil limit as no limit and a limit of `0` as no credit (every order over zero lands over it). The account summary, the portal dashboard and the partner dashboard read a `NULL` limit as `0`, as they always showed for no limit, and keep their own wires (the partner dashboard stays float dollars; `GET /api/v1/accounts/{id}` keeps cents) |
| C2-1 | customer: the jobs merge (quote `job_id`, pricing rules, the portal's projects) | Two job tables: `customer_jobs` (written by the seed, read by nothing) and `projects` (the portal, orders, quotes); `quotes.job_id` referenced `customer_jobs`; `pricing_rules.job_id` referenced `customer_jobs` | The job is `projects` (ADR 0005 section 7.1) and migration 091 loses no row. Every `customer_jobs` row with a customer is copied into `projects` with its id (`Active` or `Inactive` from `is_active`); a job with no customer takes the customer of the quotes that name it when exactly one customer's quotes do. A job that still has no single customer (none names it, or the quotes of several customers do) cannot be a project (`projects.customer_id` is NOT NULL): it is kept whole in `customer_jobs_unmigrated` and the migration reports the count in a `RAISE NOTICE`. A quote takes `project_id = job_id` when its `project_id` is empty and its job was copied. A quote whose job was not copied, or whose `project_id` already names another project, keeps its project as it is and its job link in `quote_jobs_unmigrated` (`quote_id`, `job_id`, `reason` `job_not_copied` or `quote_has_other_project`); the quote's wire `job_id` then reads its project, as for every quote, and not the old job. A pricing rule scoped to a job that was not copied is moved out whole into `pricing_rules_unmigrated` (it prices nothing, and is not widened to every job; nulling its `job_id` would collide on the rule scope key). The list of copied ids is kept in `customer_jobs_copied`. `quotes.job_id` and `customer_jobs` are dropped and `pricing_rules.job_id` references `projects`. The down file restores every job, each quote's link and each rule from those tables. The quote module reads and writes `project_id` as its wire `job_id`, so quotes whose job was copied carry the same `job_id` as before. The demo seed writes the customers' jobs into `projects`, so the portal's project list and its quote `project_name` now show them (goldens `portal_projects` and `portal_quotes`) |

| C2-2a | order: `POST /api/v1/orders` | Decoded the full legacy body with unknown fields ignored (`quote_id` aside, any value); no validation (a zero quantity or negative price was a 500 or stored); a bad product or customer a 500; no `Location`; no event; the client sent the price per sale unit in cents | The create builds a draft priced by the platform rules: a stocked product line without a price takes the pricing engine's answer (`price_source price_list`); a price beside the engine's is an override that names its `override_reason` (1 to 500 characters; a price without a reason is a 400 naming `lines[i].override_reason`, and a price equal to the engine's is stored as `price_list` with the reason dropped), stored with the engine's answer beside it and one `audit_log` row (`order.line_price_overridden`) in the transaction; a charge line names a `charge_code` and takes its default price when it sends none (`manual`); a non stock product line sends its own price (`manual`); a text line carries only a description (a priced field on it is a 400). A discount is one of `discount_percent` or `discount_cents`, never both, either with `discount_reason` (each rule a 400 naming the field), and writes one `order.line_discounted` audit row. A stocked line is sold in its product's stocking unit (`unit_not_stock_unit` 409 otherwise, until cycle 3). A kit product explodes into `kit` plus `component` lines once, at create and edit. The conversion pair follows ADR 0001 7a with the cycle 2 rule: when `price_uom` equals `uom` the pair is 1 and 1 (any other pair on equal units is a 400 on `lines[i].uom_qty`); the rule and the rule that a different `price_uom` needs a pair run on the resolved units, after a stocked line's unit is filled from the product. A `branch_id` is held to the caller's branch context (ADR 0007 2.3; the portal checkout names the customer's branch as a trusted in process caller). `quote_id` is not accepted (a 400 naming it): a quote's order is made by `POST /api/v1/quotes/{id}/convert`. 201 with `Location: /api/v1/orders/{id}`, the `ETag`, and `order.created` as the transaction's last statement |

A line keeps its id across edits when the request sends it. The tax estimate is refreshed on every edit. `branch_id`, `quote_id` and `currency` are fixed at create (a PUT that sends them is a 400). Writes `order.updated` |
| C2-2a | order: `POST /api/v1/orders/{id}/transitions` | No such route; the lifecycle was `POST /{id}/confirm`, `/{id}/fulfill` and `/{id}/cancel` (bare 204s; a credit hold answered 500 after writing `ON_HOLD` outside any transaction) | The transitions route with ADR 0005 5.2's table. `draft` to `confirmed` runs the guards (`po_required`, `contact_authority`, `empty_order`, the price exposure gate, `tax_rate_not_configured`) and the credit check of 5.3 (exposure read from the documents, never `customers.balance_due`): over the limit the order lands `on_hold` in the same transaction with `hold_reason credit_limit` and the `order.hold` event, and the answer is 200 with the held order, never an error; under it the ship-to is snapshotted, `confirmed_at` set and `order.confirmed` written. `on_hold` to `confirmed` is the release (roles admin, owner, finance; a sales role gets 403), skips the credit check, writes `order.hold_released`, then `order.confirmed` only when the order was never confirmed before (a release of a manual hold writes `order.hold_released` alone). `draft` to `on_hold` is refused (409 `invalid_state_transition`). `order.hold`, `order.hold_released` and `order.reopened` carry `from_status`. `confirmed`/`backordered` to `on_hold` is a manual hold and requires `hold_note`. Any live status to `draft` reopens (`order.reopened`; refused with `has_fulfilments` once the order has been billed). Anything outside the table is 409 `invalid_state_transition`. A confirm with a configured tax provider prices the tax before its transaction and refuses with `tax_quote_stale` when the lines moved between the price and the lock; a provider failure is 503 before any row is written. From C2-2b the confirm allocates each stocked line `min(available, quantity)` from the order's branch (a kit's components in whole kits) and the rest lands on back order: the order is `backordered`, with `order.backordered` after `order.confirmed`; the release of a credit hold allocates an order that never was; a cancel, a reopen and a close short give the allocation back. | C2-2a | order: `POST /api/v1/orders/{id}/confirm`, `POST /api/v1/orders/{id}/fulfill`, `POST /api/v1/orders/{id}/cancel` | The lifecycle actions as bare 204 routes (the credit hold path a 500) | Removed. The base URL semantics are unchanged (omitted leaves the override, empty clears it, a bad URL is a 400 writing nothing). A saved key reaches the AI stack's key stores within their 30 second cache TTL (the module writes the rows in its transaction; the stores are the read side) |

| C3-1 | pricing: `GET /api/v1/pricing/calculate` | `{product_id, original_price, final_price, discount_pct, source, details}` in float dollars with an UPPERCASE `source`; the engine rounded a rule or category price to cents and left a tier or level price as a raw float product; a `quantity` that was not a positive number silently became 1 and a `job_id` that was not a UUID was ignored; unknown parameters ignored | `{customer_id, product_id, quantity, uom, unit_price_ten_thousandths, price_uom, uom_qty, price_uom_qty, line_total_cents, price_basis, details}` (ADR 0006 7.3): the exact scale 4 price in the product's stocking unit (until C3-2A-pricing), the conversion pair (`"1"` and `"1"`), the extension rounded once to cents by the line extension rule, and `price_basis` lowercase (`contract`, `tier`, `retail`, `quantity_break`, `job_override`, `promotional`, `category_tier`, `category_account`). The engine is exact (`math/big`): a derived price (a percent off, a markup, a margin, a tier or level multiplier) is rounded once, to scale 4, half away from zero (10 percent off 1.3725 is 1.2353, where the base answered 1.24); a fixed price is returned as stored. A `quantity` that is not a positive decimal string at scale 4 is a 400 naming it, a `job_id` that is not a UUID a 400 naming it, and any other parameter a 400 `unsupported_query_parameter`. Golden `pricing` re-recorded |

The float64 fields carry the exact scale 4 value (never a cent rounding): a rule, category or tier price differs from the base by less than one cent, the volume ladder's saving is the exact scale 4 difference (a 12.345 percent break on 1.0000 saves 0.1234, where the base answered 0.12). `CalculateScaled` is added and answers an `httpx.Price`. The AI load management golden and the integration golden are byte for byte unchanged. Three further effects, named on purpose: a quantity that is not a number (NaN, infinite) or lies beyond 99999999.9999 is an error from the engine where the base priced NaN as zero and the oversized quantity as given (the counter adapter and the portal swallow the error and fall back to the base price, as they always did on an engine error); a derived price beyond 99999999.9999 is an error, never a wrapped value; and the volume ladder no longer lists a rung whose saving is zero after exact rounding (62 rungs the base advertised on the seed carried `saves_per_unit` 0, a cent rounding artefact of the same price), while a rung with a real sub cent saving is now listed (8 on the seed) |

The DELETE takes the revision and a missing rule is 404. | C3-1 | pricing: `POST /api/v1/pricing/category-rules/bulk` elements that carry an `id` | An element with an `id` replaced that rule with no revision check (last write won), rows were locked in input order, and the audit entry recorded the element's scope rather than the stored row's | An element that names the `id` of an existing rule must carry the `revision` it read (428 without, 409 `stale_revision` when stale; a `revision` without an `id` is a 400) and may not change the rule's `target_type`, `customer_id`, `tier` or `category_id` (400 naming `id`). The same rule twice in one batch is a 400 naming the second element's `[i].id` (the batch wrote nothing). Rows are locked in id order, so two batches naming the same rules in opposite orders cannot deadlock. A new `id` that does not exist still inserts. The audit entries' `new_values` come from the stored rows after the write, so they carry what the batch really left, the moved `revision` included, never the element the client sent (the audit trail gained the `revision` key on old and new values alike) || C3-1 | kit components: `PUT /api/v1/products/{id}/kit-components` | The replace ran as separate autocommit statements (a fault between them left the kit half written or emptied), ignored the product revision this item gives products (no precondition; two concurrent PUTs interleaved), wrote no audit row and no event, and accepted one component twice in a list | The replace is ONE transaction: the product row is locked `FOR UPDATE` and its revision checked inside the transaction (`If-Match` strong or weak, or the body `revision`; neither is 428 `precondition_required`, stale 409 `stale_revision`), the delete, the inserts and the `is_kit` update with the revision bump are one database act, and the audit row and a `product.updated` event (parts `kit_components`) are written in the same transaction, the event last, so a fault partway leaves the kit, the flag, the revision, the audit row and the event exactly as they were. The body takes `revision`; a component listed twice is a 400 naming `components[i].component_product_id`. The answer and the GET carry the product's current `revision`, and the PUT's 200 carries its `ETag` |
| C3-1 | `GET /api/v1/events`: the C3-1 modules' event vocabulary | No product, location, pricing rule, category or category rule events existed; only the category rules carried an audit trail, in their own table, and no other catalog write wrote an audit row or an event | The feed carries `product.created` and `product.updated` (data `sku`, `revision`, and `parts` naming what moved: `margins`, `dimensions`, `lead_time`, `kit_components`, `average_cost`, `reorder_targets`), `location.created`, `location.updated` and `location.archived` (`code`, `type`, `revision`), `pricing_rule.created` (`name`, `rule_type`, `revision`), `category.created` and `category.updated` (`slug`, `name`, `path`), and `category_rule.created`, `category_rule.updated` and `category_rule.deleted`, each with entity `{kind, id}`. Every one of these writes also records an `audit_log` row in the same transaction (a category rule keeps its own `category_pricing_audit` trail and gains the event), the event last; a failed audit or event write rolls the write back. The system writers (the receipt's average cost, the reorder scheduler's targets) write through the same seam, inside the caller's transaction when it holds one. The `events` golden's first page moves with the new writes (the location events land first); the customer events stay pinned by the customer group's own types-filtered steps |
| C2-2b | order: new `POST /api/v1/orders/{id}/fulfillments` | No such route; `POST /{id}/fulfill` was a bare 204 that allocated the whole order in one act, invoiced its pre tax total as one invoice and never reduced stock by line | The money moment (ADR 0005 5.6): one transaction that moves stock out of the allocation (`exceeds_allocation` 409 above it), builds the invoice through the shared line rules (each billed extension is the difference of two cumulative totals, so the invoices of a line sum to the order line's total to the cent, an amount discount prorated), posts the invoice entry with its cost of goods sold (DR `1020` total and `5010` cost; CR each revenue account, `2020` tax, `1030` cost) and the AR subledger debit, updates the lines and status and writes `invoice.created` then `order.partially_fulfilled` or `order.fulfilled`. A pickup order requires `picked_up_by` (1 to 200 characters). 201 with the order and `Location: /api/v1/invoices/{id}`. An order may have many invoices |
| C2-2b | order: new `POST /api/v1/orders/{id}/allocate` | No such route | The desk's retry for a back order: allocates what is available to the order's back ordered lines, derives the status, writes `order.backorder_released` when it clears. Never touches the request queue |
| C2-2b | order: new `GET /api/v1/orders/fulfillment-requests`, `POST /api/v1/orders/fulfillment-requests/{delivery_id}/retry` | No such routes | The fulfilment request queue of ADR 0005 5.5, for admin, owner, finance and warehouse: the list envelope (filters `parked`, `order_id`; oldest first) with each request's attempts and `last_error`, and the retry that clears a parked request |

| C2-2b | delivery: `POST /api/v1/delivery/deliveries`; integration `POST /api/integration/delivery-routes` | Accepted any order | A pickup (will-call) order is refused: 409 with the blocker `pickup_order` (the delivery route in the platform envelope; the integration seam in its own `{error}` shape, `a pickup order is never routed: order <id>`) |

| C2-2b | goldens that follow | `order`, `invoice` (the script now confirms through `If-Match`, where its revision in the body was a 400 that left the order a draft, and fulfils through the fulfilments route instead of seeding an invoice row; the allocate, fulfil and request queue routes are scripted), `account`, `clockwindow`, `document_invoice`, `gl_accounts_entries`, `reporting_*` (the fulfilment's real invoice, entry and subledger debit), `delivery_delivered`, `portal_billing`, `portal_catalog`, `pim_media`, `purchase_order_flow`, `purchase_order_list` | The order scripts that go onto a route are delivery orders (a pickup order is never routed); a confirm now allocates (portal available quantity, allocated totals); delivery completion queues a request instead of building an invoice (the worker is not part of the golden run); the auto purchase orders carry their lines |

Items are `InvoiceSummary`, the head of the single read. The branch wall applies to the list and its count (a context branch, else the caller's grants, else every branch for an administrator or an unbound key) |
| C2-3 | invoice: every invoice body (`GET /api/v1/invoices/{id}`, the list items, the void answer) | `status` UPPERCASE (`UNPAID`, `PARTIAL`, `PAID`, `VOID`, `OVERDUE`); `subtotal`, `tax_amount`, `total_amount` cents; `tax_rate` a float fraction; `payment_terms` a free text string; `due_date` a timestamp; `lines` omitted when empty, each `{id, invoice_id, product_id, product_sku, product_name, quantity (float), price_each (cents per sale unit), created_at}`; Overdue is computed (open and the due date before the branch's today): the invoice's `is_overdue` and the list's `overdue` filter. Every reader lists the open statuses as `UNPAID` and `PARTIAL` (`invoice.OpenInvoiceStatuses`: the order credit exposure, the dashboard, reporting aging, the customer delete guard, the portal balance). The portal's past due total now counts `UNPAID` and `PARTIAL` invoices past their due date (a partly paid invoice past due was left out before, which is what the OVERDUE rows that migrate to PARTIAL would otherwise lose) |
| C2-3 | invoice: new `POST /api/v1/invoices/{id}/transitions` (the void) | No such route; no invoice void existed (nothing deleted an invoice and nothing reversed one) | Body `{"to": "void", "revision": n, "reason": "..."}` (`If-Match` or the body revision: neither is 428, a stale one 409 `stale_revision`; a void needs a reason, 400 naming it; roles `admin`, `owner`, `finance`, a sales caller is 403). Any other target is 409 `invalid_state_transition` (`unpaid`, `partial`, `paid` and `written_off` are derived from payments and credit memos, never sent), a word that is no status a 400. Refused with the blocker `has_applications` while the invoice is not unpaid, a payment is recorded against it or an applied credit memo names it (C2-4 turns the check into live applications), and with `has_credit_memos` while a credit memo that is not void names it; a deposit applied to the invoice counts as an application; an invoice with money and no journal entry (neither its `gl_entry_id` nor a posted entry naming it) is 409 `no_ledger_entry`, and an invoice of an order closed short is 409 `order_closed_short` (the closed remainder must not come back to life). One transaction in ADR 0005 section 11's order: the invoice's order row and the customer's credit serialization first, then the invoice row, the stock back on hand and the order (`quantity_fulfilled` of each billed order line reduced, allocation re-run for exactly those quantities, what stock cannot cover going back on back order, the status re-derived: a fulfilled order becomes `confirmed` or `backordered`), the customer row, the invoice's whole journal entry reversed (`REVERSAL`, dated the void date in the branch's calendar, in the invoice's currency) and the subledger credited back (a `REVERSAL` row), the audit row, then `invoice.voided` last (and `order.backordered` when the order cannot re-allocate). Create (`POST`, 201, `Location`, `ETag`) makes a draft: `number` null (the gapless `CM-` number is minted at post, so a voided draft consumes none), `status` `draft`, lines built and totalled with NEGATIVE quantities, line totals, `subtotal_cents`, `tax_cents`, `total_cents` and `open_cents` (a plain sum is the AR effect); `currency` is the invoice's or the customer's effective currency, copied, a `currency` in the body is a 400; `branch_id` is held to the caller's branch like every payload branch (403 naming it); the customer, job and ship-to are checked to belong together. A line that names an invoice line (`invoice_line_id`) sends only the negative quantity, `restock` and an optional description and takes its price, pair and discount from the invoice line, in proportion to what was charged so the credit memos of a line sum to its total to the cent; it cannot credit more than the line billed less what earlier credit memos returned (409 blocker `exceeds_billed`, drafts counted at draft time). A free line (a price adjustment, a fee given back, a note) names a product, a charge code or only a description with its own price. Tax is at the invoice's rate on the memo's taxable base, never more than the invoice's tax less the tax earlier posted memos credited, the memo that returns the last of the taxable amount taking exactly the remainder (a provider's invoice credits in proportion to its tax); a memo with no invoice resolves the rate like an order (exemption, ship-to, branch; none configured is 409 `tax_rate_not_configured`). List: the envelope newest first, filters `status`, `customer_id`, `invoice_id`, `job_id`, `include=total`, scoped `credit_memos.created_at_id_desc`. Writes `credit_memo.created` and `credit_memo.updated` |

A failed event write rolls each act back |
| C2-3 | credit memo: migration 097 | `credit_memos` had `id`, `invoice_id`, `customer_id`, `amount` (`NUMERIC(10,2)`), `reason`, `status` (`PENDING`, `APPLIED`, `VOID`), `created_at`, `applied_at` | Per ADR 0005 13 (C2-3): `PENDING` becomes `DRAFT` (unnumbered), `APPLIED` stays `APPLIED` when it names an invoice and becomes `OPEN` when it names none (the old apply already lowered the subledger, so an open credit keeps documents and subledger agreeing), `VOID` stays; `total_amount = -amount`; one `ADJUST` charge line each (quantity -1 `EA`, unit price the amount, untaxed); `currency` and `branch_id` from the invoice or the customer; `memo_date`; every memo not in `DRAFT` numbered `CM-` in `(created_at, id)` order through the counter; `revision`, the void columns, `reason_code` `OTHER`; the new table `credit_memo_lines`; the CHECKs (totals not positive, a draft has no number and a posted memo has one). `credit_memos.amount` is kept, equal to `-total_amount`, for the raw readers |

| C2-3 | order: fulfilment invoices (`POST /api/v1/orders/{id}/fulfillments`) | The invoice had no number and the customer row was locked after the invoice insert (by the subledger post); a zero total invoice was `UNPAID`; `invoice.created` carried no number; the invoice line's `discount_cents` on a partial piece was the discount prorated on its own and could differ by a cent from the line total split | The customer row is locked and the gapless number minted immediately before the insert (section 11 steps 7 and 8); a zero total invoice is created `paid` with `paid_at` and writes `invoice.paid` after `invoice.created`; `invoice.created` carries `number`; the invoice carries the customer's terms snapshot; each invoice line's `discount_cents` is its gross piece less its net piece (`salesdoc.BilledDiscount`), so total plus discount equals the piece's gross extension to the cent and the shares over any partial invoices sum to the order line's discount exactly; the line also carries the order line's `priced_unit_price`, `override_reason` and `price_adjusted_by`; an invoice entry dated into a closed fiscal period is 409 with the blocker `period_closed` (it was a 500), as the void's reversal and a credit memo's post are |

| C2-3 | goldens that follow | `invoice` scripted the legacy list, get and credit memo routes; `portal_billing` read `data[0]` of the legacy list and seeded an invoice with `payment_terms`; `account`, `clockwindow`, `reporting_reports` and `gl_accounts_entries` held the seed's numbers; `document_invoice` held the PDF's text | `invoice` is re-scripted on the new routes (the cursor list and its filters and refusals, the derived status refusal, the void refusals, a credit memo from draft through edit, post and void, the invoice and credit memo events read back from `/api/v1/events`); `portal_billing` follows the envelope and gains `number` and `is_overdue`; `account` (the credit memo post and void are a net zero pair of subledger rows in place of the old -5.00 applied memo), `clockwindow` and `reporting_reports` (the partly paid seed invoices add payments), `gl_accounts_entries` (the credit memo post and void write two entries ahead of the group, shifting entry numbers by two) and `document_invoice` (the PDF layout) move accordingly. An administrator that sends `X-Branch-Id` is held to that branch, as before. The per customer read behind the partner surface takes the same predicate with no behaviour change there (partner routes run without the branch middleware, so both scope parameters are nil as before) |
| SEC-branch | quotes: `GET /api/v1/quotes/analytics` | The three analytics queries (the 90 day state counts and values, the average days to close, the 30 day trend) read `FROM quotes` with no branch predicate, so any caller the route admits reported every branch's totals, values and trend, with or without `X-Branch-Id`, while the same module's list and record reads were held to the caller's branches | All three queries take the same three arm predicate as the list (the trend's lives in its join, so a day with no matching quote still reports its zero row): a context branch counts its own quotes; with no context branch a bound non-admin user counts the branches granted to the user, none granted counting none; an administrator without a header, an unbound key and the single-branch switch count every branch's, as before. An administrator that sends `X-Branch-Id` is held to that branch |
| SEC-branch | inventory: `GET /api/v1/inventory?product_id=` | The route's query had no branch predicate at all (not even the context branch idiom): every caller holding the warehouse role read every branch's stock rows for the product, with or without `X-Branch-Id`, while the module's adjust and transfer writes were held to the caller's branches | The query is scoped to the caller's branches: a context branch sees its own rows; with no context branch a bound non-admin user sees the rows of the branches granted to the user, none granted listing none; an administrator without a header, an unbound key, the single-branch switch, AUTH_MODE=dev and callers with no branch context at all (the portal catalog calls the same repository read with no middleware) see every branch's, as before. An administrator that sends `X-Branch-Id` is held to that branch. A legacy inventory row with no `location_id` has no branch on its joined location and matches no scoped arm: it stays visible to an administrator without a header and to callers with no branch context only, and C4-1's migration of legacy stock rows onto locations retires the case |
| SEC-branch | purchase orders: `GET /api/v1/purchase-orders/recommendations` | The route runs behind the branch middleware, so the inventory list it reads stock from was held to the caller's branches by this PR, but its sales velocity read (order_lines joined to orders) had no branch predicate: a bound purchasing user held to one branch compared its own branches' stock against every branch's demand, inflating urgency and suggested quantities, and a user with no grants saw no branch's stock against full company demand | Both reads behind the route take the same three arm predicate, so stock and demand always cover the same branch set: a context branch's stock and that branch's sales; with no context branch the stock and sales of the branches granted to the user, none granted listing no stock and no real sales (a product with no visible sales keeps the synthetic velocity proxy, at zero visible stock); an administrator without a header, an unbound key and the single-branch switch read every branch's stock and demand, as before. Reorder targets are stored on the products table (reorder_point and reorder_qty are per product, not per product and branch), so the manual trigger `POST /api/v1/purchase-orders/refresh-reorder-targets` and the background scheduler share the same compute-from-every-branch path: the scheduler calls `RefreshReorderTargets` with `context.Background` and no branch context (arm 3 fires, every branch's sales flow), and the handler strips the `BranchContext` the branch middleware put on the request and marks the resulting context as a system caller before calling the same service, so a bound purchasing user's refresh writes the same targets the scheduler and an administrator's would. | C5-1b | crm: `GET /api/v1/customers/{customerId}/activities` | A bare array of every activity ordered by activity_date DESC; every query parameter was silently ignored; `activity_type` was the storage spelling (CALL, MEETING, EMAIL, NOTE); optional fields were omitted when unset; a customer behind the caller's branch wall listed its activities anyway through no wall of the route's own | The list envelope of ADR 0001 section 1, newest first on `(created_at, id)` with the opaque cursor; `activity_type` (lowercase vocabulary only, an uppercase value is a 400) and `contact_id` filter, `include=total` counts; a parameter the route does not declare is a 400 `unsupported_query_parameter`; every read and lock carries the branch wall through the activity's customer (`customer_branches`), so a caller held to another branch sees an empty page; errors are the one envelope. `crm_activities.created_at` is NOT NULL and revision exists (migration 096), which also fills a NULL `activity_date` from the backfilled `created_at` and sets it NOT NULL, and normalises the stored `activity_type` before constraining it: every value is uppercased and trimmed (a lowercase `call` and a padded `  meeting  ` become their storage spellings), whatever is still outside the four storage values (an empty string, a mixed-case word, a NULL on a schema that drifted nullable) maps to `NOTE`, and a CHECK holds the column to the four values, so the list's exact-match `activity_type` filter and the wire's closed vocabulary agree with what is stored |

| C5-1b | crm: `PUT /api/v1/activities/{id}` | A full replace with no concurrency check (two editors silently overwrote each other); the handler echoed the decoded body, so omitted fields read back zero values and `customer_id` came back as the zero UUID; a body `customer_id` was silently ignored; a bad `activity_type` was a plain text 400; no audit row, no event | The write needs the client's revision (`If-Match` or a body `revision`: neither is 428 `precondition_required`, a stale one 409 `stale_revision`, `*` or a list 400, header and body disagreeing 400); the check and the write are one transaction (the row is locked first); the response is the stored row at its new revision with the `ETag`; a body `customer_id` is a 400 naming it; the audit row `activity.updated` and the event `activity.updated` are written in the transaction, the event last |

| C5-1b | migration 096 `crm_projects_millwork_wire_contract` (operational, no route) | `crm_activities`, `projects` and `millwork_options` had a nullable `created_at` (the lists' keyset ordering reads it, and a nullable ordering column has no cursor position, ADR 0001 section 2), no `revision` (nothing to check an `If-Match` against), an unconstrained `crm_activities.activity_type`, and no keyset indexes | Every backfill and its source: `crm_activities.created_at` from `activity_date`, then `updated_at`, then `NOW()` (the row's own times before the migration's clock), set NOT NULL; `projects.created_at` and `millwork_options.created_at` from `updated_at`, then `NOW()`, set NOT NULL; `crm_activities.activity_date` NULL to `created_at` (already backfilled above, so a legacy row lands on its own recorded time, never the migration's clock), set NOT NULL, because the wire reads it into a non-nullable value and a NULL failed the scan for the whole feed; `millwork_options.price_adjustment` NULL to `0` (the identity of an adjustment and the column's own default; the wire scales it to cents in SQL and a NULL failed the scan for the whole category's list), set NOT NULL; `crm_activities.description` NULL to `''` (the value the old Go zero value already wrote on the wire), set NOT NULL; `crm_activities.activity_type` normalised in two rewrites (every value `upper(btrim(...))`, then everything still outside the four storage values, a NULL included, mapped to `NOTE`) and held to the four by a CHECK. `revision BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 1` on all three tables: every existing row starts at 1, so the precondition has a value to check from the first write. Three keyset indexes, each leading with its list's scope column: `crm_activities (customer_id, created_at DESC, id DESC)`, `projects (customer_id, created_at DESC, id DESC)`, `millwork_options (category, created_at DESC, id DESC)`. The down file drops the revision columns, the CHECK, the two new NOT NULLs and the three indexes, and does not restore: `created_at` stays NOT NULL (a NULL ordering column cannot come back without inventing times), a description backfilled from NULL stays `''`, an `activity_date` filled from `created_at` and a `price_adjustment` filled with `0` keep their filled values, and a normalised `activity_type` stays normalised (which value a row carried is not recorded) |
| C5-1b | migration 102 `crm_projects_millwork_updated_at` (operational, no route) | `crm_activities`, `projects` and `millwork_options` had a nullable `updated_at` (each module's repository scans it into a non-nullable `time.Time`, so a single row with `updated_at = NULL` failed the scan and 500ed the whole list: `GET /api/v1/activities/{id}`, `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/activities`, `GET /api/portal/v1/projects`, `GET /api/portal/v1/projects/{id}` and `GET /api/v1/millwork/options`) | For each of the three tables, `updated_at` NULL is backfilled to `created_at` (NOT NULL by 096, so a row lands on its own recorded time, never the migration's clock) and the column is set NOT NULL, so a later raw insert cannot reintroduce the 500. No new index, no new constraint; a row that never held NULL is unchanged. The down restores the nullable shape; the filled values stay filled (which row held a NULL is not recorded), and a re-up is idempotent |
| C3-1b | inventory: `GET /api/v1/inventory` | A bare array of the product's rows (JSON `null` when empty), float quantities with no unit and no `available`, the deprecated `location` text, `product_id` required (a missing value 400, a value that was not a UUID 500), unknown parameters silently ignored, no paging | The levels list of ADR 0006 7.2 on the ADR 0001 envelope: `items`, `next_cursor`, `limit` (plus `total` under `include=total`), keyset paged on `created_at DESC, id DESC` (migration 098 gave the table the column). Every quantity is a scale 4 decimal string in the product's stocking unit with `available` (`quantity - allocated`) and `uom` beside it; `location_id` and `location_name` (the location's path, else the row's legacy text) replace the deprecated `location`; the product summary (`id`, `sku`, `description`, `stock_uom`) is embedded in every row under `include=product` and only then. `product_id` and `location_id` are optional filters that filter (no `product_id` is the whole levels list); unknown parameters, a repeated filter, a non-UUID filter value, a bad `limit`, a malformed or foreign-ordering cursor and an unknown `include` name are 400s naming the field in the ADR 0001 error envelope. Goldens `inventory` and `inventory_transfer` re-recorded |
| C3-1b | inventory: the levels list's branch wall and its count | PR 44 scoped the list query to the caller's branches (a context branch its own rows; no context branch, a bound non-admin user the granted branches, none granted none; an administrator without a header, an unbound key, the single-branch switch and callers with no branch context every branch's; a legacy row with no `location_id` visible to the last two only) | The same three-arm predicate, statement for statement, in the page query and in the `include=total` count, which is new: the wall now holds on the count too. | C3-1b | migration 098 `inventory_read_contract` (operational, no route) | The inventory table had no creation timestamp, only the `updated_at` every stock move rewrites, and `updated_at` was nullable | `created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()` added, backfilled from each row's `updated_at` (the earliest time the row itself can name; a NULL `updated_at` is filled first), `updated_at` made NOT NULL, and the keyset index `(created_at DESC, id DESC)` for the converted list.
