| C2-3 | payment: `POST /api/v1/payments/card` when the invoice refuses a charge the gateway approved | A 409 whose body message was the generic `Conflict`; the reversal's result was only logged, and a reversal that failed answered the same 409 | The 409 body message now states which reversal happened (`the card charge was voided` or `refunded`). When the void and the refund both fail the route answers 502, code `CHARGE_NOT_REVERSED`, message saying the card was charged and finance must reconcile the named gateway transaction id (the id only). Every reversal writes an audit row `payment.charge_reversal` on the invoice (gateway transaction id, amount, outcome; no card data). The reversal runs detached from the request, with its own timeout, and also runs when the request ended before the transaction could open |
| GOLDEN-clock | the characterisation goldens (harness only, no route and no product behaviour changes) | The seed day was the UTC day while invoice dates are the branch's local date, so between UTC midnight and the branch zone's midnight the invoice, `gl_accounts_entries` and `delivery_fleet` goldens drifted a day; the seeded drivers' hardcoded `cdl_expiry` and `hire_date` moved one day per calendar day against the seed day; route ETAs (now plus minutes) crossed UTC midnight near 23:59 without the end-of-run guard firing | The harness puts every branch on `Etc/UTC` before the fixtures and the scenarios; driver `cdl_expiry` and `hire_date` normalise to `<ts>` like the vehicle fixture dates; route `eta` and `estimated_arrival` normalise to `<eta>`. Re-recorded, and only for those reasons: `delivery_fleet` (driver dates), `delivery_route_lifecycle` (ETA placeholders), `location` and `location_item` (the seeded branches' `timezone` reads `Etc/UTC` where it read `America/Vancouver` or `America/New_York`; the branches the script creates with an explicit zone keep it) |
| SEC-AUDIT-PATH | refusal audit row of a refused machine-key request whose path holds a NUL byte or invalid UTF-8 (for example `GET /api/v1/admin/%00`) | The 403 stood but the row failed to write: json.Marshal escaped the NUL to the JSON escape sequence `\u0000`, the jsonb parser rejected it with SQLSTATE 22P05 ("unsupported Unicode escape sequence"), and a NUL in a text column answered 22021 ("invalid byte sequence"), so the audit trail of a refused request was lost whenever the URL held a NUL | The audit writer is the one place that prepares every string going into an audit_log row for Postgres text and jsonb, at every depth of the changes map and in every plain string column: a NUL byte is replaced with the visible marker `\u0000` (the JSON escape spelled out as literal text), invalid UTF-8 is replaced with U+FFFD, and the marshalled JSON for the changes is rewritten once so no nested string ever carries the JSON escape the jsonb parser rejects. A refusal whose path needed sanitising carries `path_sanitised: true` in its `changes` (the marker itself is not unique; the server log line carries the verbatim path). The 512 byte cap on the stored path is applied after sanitising, so a NUL does not push the row over the cap. Per-caller code keeps the verbatim value; the row stays whole |
| SEC-AUDIT-PATH | `RequiredScopeForPath` on an admin path whose second segment is "." or ".." (for example `/api/v1/admin/./settings/ai`, `/api/v1/admin/x/../settings/ai`, `/api/v1/admin/staff/../settings/ai`) for machine keys on `/api/v1/admin` only | The router's 307 redirect answered, the redirected request was authorised afresh on its own clean path, and the ADR 0009 guarantee "coarse scopes reach only routes no area declares" leaned on the router cleaning the path. No scope was bypassed; the guarantee rested on the router, not on the auth layer | Hardening: the auth layer refuses (the most restrictive answer, alongside the empty second segment already refused) before the scope check, so the guarantee is held by the layer that owns it. A dirty segment or any path not equal to path.Clean(path) returns 403 with the standard envelope. The clean path is unchanged (admin:settings still reaches `/api/v1/admin/settings/ai`, admin:read is still refused on it). A person on a dirty admin path is not covered: the desk uses user JWTs and never reaches the machine key middleware, so the mux answers its 307 (or 404 for the encoded dot forms and the trailing slash) exactly as on the base branch. See `docs/adr/0009-finer-admin-scopes.md` section 5 |
| SEC-CHARGECODE-GUARD | `POST /api/v1/charge-codes`, `PUT /api/v1/charge-codes/{id}` | The write guard serve passed (admin, owner, finance) was ignored: `chargecode.RegisterRoutes` wrapped every route in its first guard, so a sales user could create and edit charge codes, and with them the revenue account a charge line posts to, against C2-2a's row | A write takes the second guard: sales is refused with 403 on both writes; reads are unchanged (admin, owner, sales, finance). The mount moved into the branch wall (`branchWall.chargeCodes`) so `TestBranchWall_ChargeCodeWritesTakeTheFinanceGuard` drives serve's own wiring |
| C2-4 | payment: every payment body (`GET`/`POST /api/v1/payments`, the list items, the card answer, the applications answer) | A payment could not exist without an invoice: `invoice_id` and float `amount` dollars, UPPERCASE `method` (`CASH`, `CARD`, `CHECK`, `ACCOUNT` included) and a status nothing moved; no document number; no revision; gateway card fields always present as empty strings | A payment belongs to a customer (`customer_id`, `customer_name`), may name no invoice, and carries `number` (`PAY-000001`, gapless), `status` (`posted`, `voided`), `revision` with the `ETag` on every read and write, `currency`, integer `amount_cents` and `unapplied_cents` (the part held in deposits 2200), `received_on` (the branch's business date), `order_id` (a deposit against an order) and `job_id`, optional fields present as `null`. `method` is lowercase `cash`, `check`, `ach`, `other` for the counter route (`ACCOUNT` is gone: an account charge is an open invoice) and `card` on the gateway route only. The core's applications (`kind` `payment`, `discount`, `credit_memo`, `write_off`, `amount_cents`, `applied_on`, `reversed_at`) are the one record of AR being settled |
| C2-4 | payment: new `GET /api/v1/payments` | No such route: payments were read only per invoice | The list envelope `{items, next_cursor, limit}` newest first, `total` under `?include=total`; the filters `customer_id`, `status` (lowercase), `method` (lowercase) and `unapplied=true|false` filter and anything else is a 400 naming it |
| C2-4 | payment: `POST /api/v1/payments` | Body `{invoice_id, amount (float dollars), method, reference, notes}` against any invoice, a void one included; no GL entry was posted and the invoice status was moved by the module | Body `{customer_id, amount_cents, method, branch_id?, reference?, notes?, received_on?, order_id?, job_id?, applications?}`, each application `{invoice_id, amount_cents, discount_cents?}`; unknown fields, a bad method, a zero amount and a duplicate invoice in one request are 400s naming the field. Posts `DR 1010 / CR 2200` for the whole amount; each application settles its invoice in the same act with its own `DR 2200 / CR 1020` entry, subledger row, `invoice.partial`/`invoice.paid` and the payment's `unapplied_cents`; the early pay discount posts `DR 4050 / CR 1020` beside it, taken only within the invoice's `discount_due_date` and cap |
| C2-4 | payment: new `POST /api/v1/payments/{id}/applications`, `POST /api/v1/payments/{id}/refunds`, `POST /api/v1/payments/{id}/transitions` (the void) | No such routes: unapplied cash could not be applied later, a refund went through `POST /api/v1/payments/refund` against any payment (float `amount`, no precondition, a subledger row of type `PAYMENT` and never the GL), and no payment void existed | Applications and refunds need the payment's revision (`If-Match` or the body's `revision`: none is 428, a stale one 409); an application is bound to the payment's unapplied cash (`exceeds_unapplied`), the invoice's open amount (`exceeds_open_amount`), its customer (`customer_mismatch`) and its currency (`currency_mismatch`, refused in v1); a refund is bound to the unapplied amount and posts `DR 2200 / CR 1010` with a refund row. The void (finance roles) reverses every live application with its discounts, posts `DR 2200 / CR 1010` for the amount still unapplied (the amount less refunds) and ends the payment `voided`; a card payment is refused `card_payment` (it refunds through the gateway); the void reopens the invoices it had settled |
| C2-4 | payment: `POST /api/v1/payments/card` | Body `{invoice_id, token_id, amount}`; the charge was recorded even when the invoice refused it, and the 409 body message was the generic `Conflict`; failures of the unconfigured gateway answered 402 `conflict` | Body `{customer_id, token_id, amount_cents, applications?}`; the invoice a void committed meanwhile is refused before anything is recorded, and an approved charge the system refused is reversed (same day void, else refund) before the refusal returns, the refusal naming what happened to the charge; when neither the void nor the refund gives the money back the route answers 502 `charge_not_reversed` carrying the gateway transaction id and nothing else about the card (ADR 0001's table gains it); the gateway's own failures (unreachable, declined) answer 402 `payment_required` (also new to the table) |

| C2-4 | account: `GET /api/v1/accounts/{id}`, `GET /api/v1/accounts/{id}/transactions` | The summary read the stale `balance_due` float; the transactions list was a bare array of four types (`INVOICE`, `PAYMENT`, `ADJUSTMENT`, `REFUND`) | The summary is the AR core's: `currency`, `balance_cents` (int64), `credit_limit_cents` and `available_credit_cents` (`null` for no limit), `unapplied_cents` (open credit memos plus unapplied cash, negative or zero). The summary totals the buckets per currency. The statement is per currency: opening balance, every subledger row in range (filtered to a `job_id`), closing balance and the open documents. The reconciliation lists every customer whose balance, subledger and documents disagree and whether each currency's 1020 and 2200 tie, repairing nothing. The reverse (finance roles) reverses one live application (a payment's with its discounts) in one transaction: the entry reversed, the opposite subledger row, the invoice reopened and its cash back to unapplied |

The void is refused `has_applications` while any live application (payment, discount, credit memo or write off) stands: reverse them first. A credit memo's open credit applies to invoices with no ledger entry (both sides are 1020) and refunds out `DR 1020 / CR 1010`; its statuses past `open` are `partial` and `applied`, `credit_memo.reopened` on a reversal |

| C2-4 | migration 101 `payments_and_ar` (operational, no route) | `payments.invoice_id` was NOT NULL with no number, currency, status or unapplied column; `customer_deposits` and their applications were separate tables; credit memos had no `amount_open`; a legacy refund was a payments row of a `REFUND` method; no `ar_applications`; the chart held no 4050 or 5040 | Payments evolve in place: `number` `PAY-` gapless from the counter, `currency` (the invoice's, else the customer's, else the default), `status` `POSTED`/`VOIDED`, `branch_id`, `amount_unapplied`, `project_id`; each deposit becomes a payment (its applications become `ar_applications`, an uncapped one capped at the invoice's open amount with its excess an open credit memo `migrated payment excess`); a legacy overpayment lands as an open credit memo, not unapplied cash; legacy refunds become `payment_refunds` rows and consume an open credit memo first (the worked case of ADR 0005 13: 150 paid, 100 applied, 70 refunded ends with the payment at 0, the memo at 0, the invoice open 20, the subledger at +20, and a recompute of `amount_unapplied` leaves the payment at 0); `amount_open` is written on every invoice and credit memo from its live state; accounts 4050 and 5040 are seeded; the deposit tables are kept whole as `*_legacy`, read by nothing. Three choices move a migrated amount: cash a legacy record put against a void or written off invoice takes no application (such an invoice takes none, ADR 0005 9.2) and lands as an open credit memo `migrated payment excess`, as does an applied credit memo or deposit application naming one, so the customer's credit is kept and the void invoice carries no live application; only a `COMPLETE` refund counts as money returned, so a refund still `PENDING` (or any other status) leaves the application and `amount_unapplied` as they were; a deposit with no invoice named applies to the customer's invoices by room left, oldest due first, whatever the stored status, so a `PAID` invoice that no payment, memo or deposit names (which step 4 reopens as `UNPAID`) can take it. a migrated deposit application keeps its own entry only when that entry's debit total equals the application (compared in dollars, as the ledger stores its lines; an earlier draft compared cents and so kept none), and reversing one with no entry posts a new `1020` / `2200` entry for its amount. Down then up loses no row and changes no amount (its test); the down file merges a refund the migration split in two back into one row, and reads only `CM-` numbers when it returns the counter |

| C2-4 | goldens that follow | `payment` scripted the legacy record against an invoice and the refund route; `deposit` and `deposit_apply` scripted the deposit module's routes; `account` read the legacy summary and transactions; `payment_gateway` scripted the unconfigured gateway's 402s and the refund's 500; the invoice, order, customer, clock window, ship-to, portal billing, quote, reporting and GL entry goldens pinned the old money shapes | `payment` walks the design: unapplied cash without an invoice, the list's filters, applications with `If-Match` (partial, closing, refused over unapplied and over open, the discount refused), refunds, the void and the events feed; `payment_unapplied` is new (cash for another customer's invoice refused, then applied nowhere and voided); `deposit` reads a deposit as a payment, `deposit_apply` is deleted with the module; `account` walks the subledger cursor and the aging by customer, job and ship-to with its strict errors; `payment_gateway` moves to the card body and the gone refund route (a non card refund needs no gateway); `invoice`, `order`, `customer`, `customer_ship_tos`, `clockwindow`, `portal_billing`, `quote`, `reporting_reports`, `gl`, `gl_accounts_entries` re-recorded for the cents, `open_cents`, `unapplied_cents` and entry shapes (and `delivery_delivered`, whose order read gains `deposit_unapplied_cents: 0`) the core writes (the GL entries golden holds the AR acts' own entries now); `account` is re-recorded a second time because its two date requests (the past date aging and the inverted statement range) carried literal dates, which the harness records as offsets from the run's UTC day and so drifted by one every day; they now use `{today-3650}` and `{today+1}` to `{today}`, and the golden holds the same offsets on every day, with no response body changed |
| C3-2A-units | units: `GET`, `POST /api/v1/units`, `GET`, `PUT /api/v1/units/{code}` | The routes did not exist: units were a closed Postgres enum of sixteen codes used by `products.uom_primary` and `quote_lines.uom`, every other unit column was free text, and a dealer could not add a unit | The unit catalogue of ADR 0006 section 2 on the recipe: the keyset envelope ordered `units.code` with `dimension` and `is_active` filters, the error envelope, revision preconditions on the PUT (428 without, 409 stale), create with `Location`, and deactivation only (no delete). A system row's dimension and standard size are immutable (a 400 naming the field; a PUT that sends the stored standard size back unchanged changes nothing and is served), and the dimension and standard size of any unit named by a product unit set row or a line are immutable once referenced. The `units` module scope joins the machine key vocabulary (`units:read`, `units:write`; the machine key golden pins the 403 with its audit row). Migration 099 replaces the enum with the catalogue table |
| C3-2A-units | products: `GET`, `PUT /api/v1/products/{id}/units` | The routes did not exist: a product had one unit (`uom_primary`) and nothing converted | The product's unit set of ADR 0006 sections 3.1 to 3.3: the PUT replaces the whole set and the four default columns (stock, sale, price, purchase) in one transaction at the product's revision, derives a row's pair by the section 3.2 rules when the body omits it, stores sent pairs canonically and refuses a pair that disagrees with a derivation (400 naming `units[k].unit_qty` with the derived pair) or a set whose ordered pairs do not resolve within the bound (400 naming the row). The stored stocking unit is the body's `stock_uom`, never row order: a `stock_uom` with no row of the set, or whose row is sent as another pair than (1, 1), is a 400 naming `stock_uom` (a second (1, 1) row beside the stocking row is harmless). The stocking unit holds refuse with 409 conflict blockers: `stock_unit_in_use` (nonzero stock or an open order line), `price_unit_held` (a nonzero base price, a contract or a fixed price rule during the stocking unit hold of 9.1); both fire whatever the row order, and a PUT whose `stock_uom` does not change the stocking unit fires neither even when the set carries two (1, 1) rows. Until C3-2B a price unit other than the stocking unit is a 400 naming `price_uom`. The answer carries `warnings`, an array always present, naming sell rows that do not convert exactly into the stocking unit and the finer stocking unit that would fix them. A stocking unit change stores the four defaults exactly as the body names them: the write says so through the transaction local `gable.unit_set_write` flag, so a set that keeps selling in the old stocking unit keeps its `sale_uom` (the defaults dragging of the migration serves raw writers only, and the PUT's answer and the next GET agree). Rule 3's LENGTH derivation anchors a COUNT piece row (a unit whose one is one piece: PCS, EA), the stocking row when the stocking unit is one and any other piece row when it is not, so a 2x4x8 stocked in BF, LF or MBF derives its LF row through the piece (1.5 LF per BF, 1500 LF per MBF) instead of through the stocking unit. Rule 4's BOARD_MEASURE derivation through the piece anchors the same way: a multi piece COUNT row (a DOZ) or a container (a BOX) never stands in for the piece, so the board measure pair does not depend on the order the rows are sent. The PUT's answer is the same document the GET serves: it carries the board measure facts (`board_thickness_in`, `board_width_in`, `board_length_ft`, `random_length`) read from the product row the write locked |
| C3-2A-units | products: `POST /api/v1/products`, `GET /api/v1/products/{id}` and the list item | `stock_uom` was one of the sixteen enum codes; the product carried no board measure facts and no unit set | `stock_uom` is any active unit of the catalogue (a code that is not a catalogue row is a 400 naming `stock_uom`); the create accepts `board_thickness_in`, `board_width_in` (both halves or neither), `board_length_ft` and `random_length` (a random length product stocks in LF and carries no fixed length). Every product read carries `sale_uom`, `price_uom`, `purchase_uom` (equal to `stock_uom` until C3-2B), the board measure columns as decimal strings or null, `random_length`, and `units`, the product's unit set |
| C3-2A-units | quotes: `POST /api/v1/quotes`, `PUT /api/v1/quotes/{id}` (the lines) | A line's `uom` was one of the sixteen enum codes and its `price_uom` any `^[A-Z]{1,6}$` text; the conversion pair was whatever the client sent, checked only for positivity, and required whenever `price_uom` differed from `uom`; `uom` defaulted from the product's stocking unit and `price_uom` from `uom` | ADR 0006 sections 3.3 and 7.4: both units must be active catalogue units and, on a product line, rows of the product's set (sell on `uom`, price on `price_uom`, each a 400 naming its field). The pair is resolved by the server from the two rows and stored on the line; a sent pair must equal the resolved one as a ratio (400 naming `lines[i].uom_qty` with the pair to send) and the old requirement to send a pair when the units differ is gone for product lines (a line without a product still carries the client pair, or the standard size derivation of two catalogue units). `uom` defaults to the product's `sale_uom` and `price_uom` to the product's `price_uom`. Every product line stores its stocking unit and its exact stocking quantity: a quantity that does not convert exactly at scale 4 is a 400 naming `lines[i].quantity` with the nearest quantities that do (10 BF of a 2x4x14 names 9.9988 and 10.0016), and only quantities the line can hold are named: zero and anything past the 99999999.9999 bound are left out, the one holdable neighbour alone when the other cannot be held. A sent pair that equals the resolved pair as a ratio is stored as the canonical resolved pair itself (375 and 2 stores 187.5 and 1), on a product line and on a standard size derivation of a line without a product. A quantity whose stocking conversion is exact but past the quantity bound is a 400 naming `lines[i].quantity` saying the conversion is past the bound, never an exactness refusal |
| C3-2A-units | quotes: the line's `tally` (on every quote read and write) | A line carried no tally: random length lumber was quoted as one quantity with no lengths | ADR 0006 section 4: a line on a random length product may carry `tally: {rows: [{pieces, length_ft}]}` (pieces a JSON integer, the length a decimal string, at most 100 rows, one row per length); its linear feet are the line's quantity (a sent quantity that disagrees is a 400 naming `lines[i].quantity`; the quantity may be omitted), its sale unit is LF, and the cross section is snapshotted from the product. The read serves the tally with `thickness_in`, `width_in`, `rows`, `linear_feet` and `board_feet` (the one display rounding, R4.3: 200/3 reads 66.6667); the money goes through the resolved pair (150 LF at 500.00 per MBF is 5000 cents). A tally on any other line is a 400 naming `lines[i].tally`, and a read only field in a request is a 400 naming it. A tally whose rows are each within the row limits but whose linear feet sum is past the quantity bound is a 400 naming `lines[i].tally` stating the cap (99999999.9999 linear feet), never an accepted wrapped sum. Every line carries `stock_uom`, `stock_quantity` and `tally`, null where they do not apply |
| C3-2A-units | migration 099 `units_catalogue_and_sets` (operational, no route) | Units were the closed `uom_type` enum; a product had one unit; a quote line's pair was unvalidated client data | The unit catalogue replaces the enum (the three columns become TEXT with foreign keys to `units(code)`, values normalised and a value that cannot enter the catalogue aborts the migration naming it; `core migrate -report units` is the read only pre flight report, which compares against the section 2.2 seed codes as well as the live catalogue so a code the migration itself seeds is never listed as a new dealer unit), every product gains its stocking unit set row and the three default columns with the `price_uom = uom_primary` CHECK of the stocking unit hold, the quote line pairs become `product_unit_candidates` for the dealer to confirm (never applied), and the quote lines gain the tally table, the cross section columns and the stock backfill (a line that does not convert exactly keeps null stock fields and is reported). The raw writer defaults trigger keeps serving raw SQL (a stocking unit change drags the three defaults with it when the caller leaves them on the old stocking unit) but returns early for a writer that sets the transaction local `gable.unit_set_write` flag, which the unit set PUT does, because it names all four unit columns itself |
| CORE-migrate | the one binary's `core migrate` (operational, no route) | `core migrate` refused every flag (`flag provided but not defined: -report`), so the read only units pre flight report that ADR 0006 section 8, migration 099 and the C3-2A-units row name as `core migrate -report units` ran only through the old `go run ./cmd/migrate -report units` entry | `core migrate` parses its own flags: `-report units` runs the pre flight report (the same output as the old entry), no flag applies the migrations as before, and any other flag, report or argument, or `-report` with an empty value, is refused with exit 2 (`-h` prints the usage and exits 0); the old `go run ./cmd/migrate` entry parses the same way, so a flag it once ignored and migrated past is now refused; the other subcommands still refuse every flag |
| C2-5 | counter: every sale body (`GET`/`POST /api/v1/pos/transactions[/{id}]`, the items, complete and void routes, `POST /api/v1/pos/sync`) | No `number`, `revision`, `currency` or `invoice_id`; `status` was UPPERCASE; money was float dollars (`subtotal`, `tax_amount`, `total`, a tender's `amount`); lines were product rows only (`product_id` NOT NULL, quantity a JSON number, `unit_price` at scale 2, no position, type, pair, discount or audit columns); a completed sale's GL was booked after the commit, best effort, with no tax leg, and a split tender invoice was built on a fake product line | The sale carries `number` (`POS-000001`, the gapped `pos_transaction_number_seq`, backfilled by migration 105), `revision` with the `ETag`, `currency` (the customer's or the dealer default), `invoice_id` once completed, and `status` lowercase (`open`, `held`, `completed`, `voided`); every amount is `_cents` int64; the lines are the shared salesdoc shape of ADR 0005 section 2.2 (`line_type` product, kit with its component lines, charge and text; `quantity`, `uom_qty` and `price_uom_qty` decimal strings; `unit_price_ten_thousandths` at scale 4; typed `override_reason` and a `discount_percent` or `discount_cents` with `discount_reason`, each adjustment audited); the completion is one transaction (stock out, the invoice with origin POS and pickup posted through the AR core with the tax and COGS legs, one payment per cash, check or card tender applied to it, stored net of change, an ACCOUNT tender left open and subject to the credit check, the event last), so a failing event, audit or ledger write leaves no invoice, payment, stock move or entry. The tenders never read back null and a fresh sale reads `lines: []`, `tenders: []` |
| C2-5 | counter: `POST /api/v1/pos/transactions/{id}/complete` and `/{id}/void` | The complete took `{tenders: [{method, amount (float dollars)}]}` with no precondition and answered 422 on refusals the handler picked per route; the void was a bare POST with no body that reversed no ledger entry | The complete takes `{tenders: [{method: cash\|check\|card\|account lowercase, amount_cents, reference, token_id}], picked_up_by, revision}` on the sale's revision (`If-Match` or the body: 428 `precondition_required` without one, 409 `stale_revision` on a stale one) and its refusals are the error envelope with blockers `insufficient_tender`, `change_from_cash`, `walk_in_account`, `card_terminal`, `card_declined`, `tax_quote_stale`, `tax_rate_not_configured`, `credit_limit`, `insufficient_stock` (and 503 `unavailable` when the tax provider fails before any row is written). The void takes `{reason, revision}` on the same precondition, runs one transaction in section 11's lock order (the sale, its payments, its invoice), refunds a card tender through the gateway before it, and is refused once the till session closed (blocker `session_closed`: after the close it is a return) or when the sale carries no invoice (`no_invoice`) |
| C2-5 | counter: `GET /api/v1/pos/transactions` | A bare array (null when the day was empty) with `register_id`, `date` and `status` silently ignored or UPPERCASE, offset paging parameters | The list envelope `{items, next_cursor, limit}` over the register's day (the page is the whole day newest first, so `next_cursor` is null; `limit` echoes the 50 served), `register_id` and `date` filter, `status` takes one lowercase value (an uppercase or unknown value, an unknown parameter, a malformed cursor or limit is a 400 naming it), and an empty day is `[]` in the bytes |
| C2-5 | counter: `GET /api/v1/pos/products/search`, `GET /api/v1/pos/catalog` | The typeahead answered 500 (`column p.is_active does not exist`) against the real schema | Bare arrays (never null) of the counter's product facts: `product_id`, `sku`, `description`, `unit_price_cents`, `uom`, `in_stock` (a decimal string) |
| C2-5 | counter: `POST /api/v1/pos/sync` | A batch replayed through the old completion path with float dollars and UPPERCASE methods; a tax provider failure rejected the sale | The batch's items are the new line and tender shapes with a `client_id` (a replay is a duplicate, not an error); the response carries `synced_count`, `duplicate_count`, `error_count`, `pending_count` with `errors` and `pending` never null, and a sale whose tax provider call fails stays `pending` and is retried, never rejected (ADR 0005 section 3's one exception) |
| C2-5 | till: `POST /api/v1/pos/till/open`, `GET /api/v1/pos/till/current` | `opening_float` float dollars; the session read UPPERCASE status with null `expected_by_method`/`counted_by_method` maps before any sale; `current` answered 200 with a `null` body when the register had no open session | `opening_float_cents` int64; the session carries lowercase `status`, `expected_by_method` and `counted_by_method` maps that are objects from the first read (never null), `over_short_cents` and `gl_entry_id` set by the close; `current` answers 404 `not_found` ("no open till session for this register") when none is open |
| C2-5 | till: `POST /api/v1/pos/till/{id}/close`, `GET /api/v1/pos/till/{id}/report`, `GET /api/v1/pos/till/{id}/zreport`, `GET /api/v1/pos/zreports` | `counted_by_method` took UPPERCASE method keys with float dollars; the over/short was booked outside the close's transaction; the expected cash subtracted change twice | `counted_by_method` takes lowercase keys in cents; the expected cash is the session's cash payments (already net of change) plus the opening float less cash refunds, the over/short is booked to 5030 inside the close's transaction with `till.closed` last, the Z report is written once at the close, and the reports' `tendered_by_method` and `expected_by_method` are objects; the Z report lists are bare arrays, never null (an empty day is `[]`) |

| C2-5 | counter: the walk-in customer and the register's branch (behaviour, no route) | A sale that named no customer posted to a NULL customer row the AR core cannot carry; the demo seed left REG-01 on the MAIN branch, which carries no default tax rate | Migration 105 seeds the WALK-IN customer and `pos.walk_in_customer_id`; a customerless sale books to it (an ACCOUNT tender for it is refused `walk_in_account`) and prices its lines through the engine for it, never an empty id; the seed repoints REG-01 at the Kelowna branch (rate 0.12), because the counter refuses a sale or return on a branch without a rate |
| C2-5 | goldens that follow | `pos`, `pos_transactions`, `pos_sync`, `pos_returns`, `pos_till_close` scripted the legacy counter: float tenders, UPPERCASE methods, the void of an open sale, the account return with no document | The five are re-scripted and re-recorded: cents tenders and floats, the line wrapper, lowercase vocabularies, the revision preconditions (extracted, never counted by hand) with the 428 and the stale 409, the void of an open sale refused, a split tender completion (cash and check against the 12 percent branch rate) and its void, the strict query refusals, a step reading the counter's own events back from the feed, and the Z report's payload masked (it embeds the session's own ids and timestamps). `crm_activity_item`, `location_item`, `pim_media`, `pricing_category_rules`, `product_item` and `reporting_reports` were re-recorded with them: their committed versions no longer pass because the counter's sales now really consume stock and post events, and the harness now sorts a cursor envelope's `items` as it sorted the legacy `data` array (the order of three lists changed) |
| C2-5 | migration 105 `pos_wire_contract` (operational, no route) | The counter's tables were the base commit's: no revisions, numbers, currency or invoice and credit memo links, `pos_line_items.product_id` NOT NULL with no line typing, a historic ACCOUNT return carrying a GL entry with no document | The steps of ADR 0005 section 13 C2-5: revisions, `POS-` and `RTN-` numbers in `(created_at, id)` order with sequences behind the column DEFAULTs, currency from the customer or the default, `invoice_id` and `credit_memo_id` links, the shared line columns with the per type CHECKs (`product_id` NULL-able), the tender's `payment_id`, each historic ACCOUNT return migrated as an OPEN credit memo carrying the return's own entry (no new journal or subledger row), and the walk-in customer with its setting. The down drops the columns, the memo and the walk-in customer, returns `unit_price` to scale 2, and the up applies again after it. It deletes the kit, component, charge and text lines the old shape cannot hold only while no completed sale holds one: it refuses (an exception naming the count) otherwise and changes nothing (C2-5r4 row below) |
| C2-5r1 | returns: `POST /api/v1/pos/returns` bounded by the sale it names | A line naming a sale line copied the sale line's list `unit_price` (before any discount), took the request's quantity and even the request's own price, and nothing capped the cumulative return: one sold line refunded any quantity, again and again, at any price | A line that names a sale line (`line_id`) refunds at that line's own extension per unit (`line_total / quantity`, the discount already inside it) with its share of the tax the sale actually collected (the sale's tax over its taxable base, so a provider priced or exempt sale returns exactly what it charged); a client `unit_price_ten_thousandths` on a linked line is a 400 naming `lines[i].unit_price_ten_thousandths`, and a quantity that with every earlier return of the line exceeds what it sold is refused 409 with the blocker `exceeds_sold`, checked before the act and again under the sale row lock. A free line (no sale named) keeps its own price at today's branch rate |
| C2-5r1 | returns: restock cost and revenue account of a linked line | A restocking return line relieved COGS at the product's today average cost (a unit sold at one cost relieved at another after the average moved), and the memo's entry debited all revenue to 4010 whatever the line's account | A linked restocking line takes its source invoice line's `unit_cost` (found by position through the sale's invoice) and records the link on `credit_memo_lines.invoice_line_id`; the entry's revenue legs are per revenue group like the sale's, so a returned FREIGHT charge debits 4020 back. A linked charge line carries its own shape (line type and charge code on the memo line); a free line still restocks at today's average |
| C2-5r1 | void: `POST /api/v1/pos/transactions/{id}/void` after a return | The return bumped the sale's revision but left it COMPLETED, so a void sent on the fresh revision paid the whole sale out a second time and restocked the returned units again | A void of a sale with any return against it is refused 409 with the blocker `has_returns` ("return the rest instead"), checked before the gateway calls and again under the sale row lock |
| C2-5r1 | complete and void: the session inside the transaction | A sale completed into a closed till session (carrying the closed session's id, missing from its expected cash and Z report, and unvoidable for ever after), and a void racing the close left the closed session expecting cash for a voided sale | The completion and the void lock the sale's till session row FOR SHARE inside their transactions and refuse 409 `session_closed` when it is not open; the close takes the same row FOR UPDATE before it aggregates, so the acts serialize and the drawer's count is coherent either way |
| C2-5r1 | complete: the tender check against the live total | The pre-transaction check priced the branch rate only (an exempt customer paying exactly was refused), and the in-transaction check reduced to `min(tendered, total) < 0` and never refused: a provider tax above the estimate completed the sale underpaid with a negative `change_cents` and an open balance on the walk-in customer | The pre-check prices with the same resolver the transaction uses (exemption first, then the provider or the branch rate); inside the transaction, once the tax is known on the locked lines, `change = tendered - total`: a negative change is refused `insufficient_tender`, a change above the cash taken `change_from_cash`, and a negative `change_cents` is never written. The ACCOUNT credit check runs under the customer's credit advisory lock (section 11 step 1a, the order module's own key), so two concurrent ACCOUNT sales for one customer cannot both pass over the limit |
| C2-5r1 | complete, void and returns: the gateway order and refused gateway money | Every guard that gates the act ran inside the transaction, after the gateway had already charged or refunded: a stale revision void refunded the card and left the sale completed, a 428 answered with the money moved, and a transaction that failed after a gateway success left the charge or refund standing with nothing recorded | Every lock free guard (the revision, the status, the session, the returns, the caps, the credit read) runs before any gateway call, so a refusal never moves money at the terminal. A charge the transaction then refuses is reversed the C2-4 card route's way (the same day void first, the refund when the void is refused, audited `pos.card_charge_reversal` in its own committed write) and answers 502 `charge_not_reversed` when neither works; a refund the transaction refuses is recorded in a committed audit row of its own (`pos.gateway_refund_orphaned`) carrying the gateway transaction and refund ids beside a CRITICAL log |
| C2-5r1 | counter and till routes: the branch wall (behaviour, no route change) | The free return took its branch from the request's `register_id` with no check against the caller's branch, and the till report and close read their session with no branch filter: a cashier granted only branch B took a cash return out of branch A's drawer, read A's report and closed A's drawer | The register and session reads resolve through the branch wall (`branchctx`): a register or session outside the caller's branch is a 404, like the sale, void and linked return reads already were |
| C2-5r1 | returns: the walk-in customer refunded to account | A return resolved to the walk-in customer with `refund_method: account` created an open credit memo on a customer that cannot hold an ACCOUNT tender: the credit was stranded where nobody could apply it | Refused 409 `walk_in_account`, as the sale side refuses an ACCOUNT tender for the walk-in; the walk-in's returns are paid from the drawer or to the card |
| C2-5r1 | migration 105 `pos_wire_contract`, second cut (operational, no route) | `pos_return_lines` had no link to the sale line (a return could not be bounded or costed by what it returned) and its `product_id` was NOT NULL (a returned charge line was a 500); the up died midway on a legacy ACCOUNT return with no customer; the down left the credit memo counter advanced, so a down then up renumbered the migrated memo and left a gap in the gapless series | `pos_return_lines.sale_line_id` links each return line to the sale line it returns (the cap and the restock cost read it) and `product_id` is NULL-able for charge lines, both returned by the down; the up preflights and refuses a legacy ACCOUNT return with no customer before it writes anything; the down returns the credit memo counter as the C2-4 down does |
| C2-5r2 | returns: a line of a named sale must name the sale line | A return line could carry `product_id` with its own `unit_price_ten_thousandths` while the request named `original_sale_id`: the line stood outside every cap (a product line on a named sale refunded any quantity, again and again, at the client's price) | When `original_sale_id` is named, every line carries `line_id` (a line without it is a 400 naming `lines[i].line_id`); only a return that names no sale stands free on its own product and price |
| C2-5r2 | returns: the invoice line link by identity, not position | The linked return found its source invoice line by matching the sale line's stored position against the invoice's positions: a cart line removed before completion left the sale's positions gapped while the invoice numbered its own lines from zero, so the return linked (and relieved COGS at) the wrong line, or none and fell back to today's average cost | Completion stores the invoice line id it built from each sale line on the sale line itself (`pos_line_items.invoice_line_id`, written in the completion's transaction, migration 105); the linked return reads the stored id, takes that line's `unit_cost` and records it on `credit_memo_lines.invoice_line_id` |
| C2-5r2 | returns: a kit line returns its components | A returned kit line restocked the kit product, which has no inventory row: the act answered 500 and the goods could never come back through a return (without restock the components stayed out and the 1030/5010 legs never moved) | A returned kit line restocks the sale's component lines under it, each at the returned fraction of its quantity and at the component's own invoice line `unit_cost` for the 1030/5010 legs; the kit line itself carries the refund at its own price and no stock. A return line that names a component of a kit sale with `restock` is a 400 naming `lines[i].restock` (the kit line carries the return) |
| C2-5r2 | returns: repeated partial returns of a line refund no more than the line was paid | Each partial return refunded its own rounded share (`line_total x returned / sold`, the tax share rounded the same way), so repeated partial returns summed past what the sale took (three returns of one unit on a 1649 line at 146 tax refunded 1797 for 1795, and the drawer paid the difference) | The refund of each linked line is capped at the line's total less every cent earlier returns refunded, and the return that completes the line takes that remainder exactly; the tax is capped the same way against the sale's tax less the tax earlier returns took. The refunded cents per line and the tax are summed under the sale row lock beside the quantity cap, and the memo, its legs and the refund are written from that in-transaction answer |
| C2-5r2 | returns: a card refund targets the sale's own card tender | The card return refunded whatever gateway transaction the request's `gateway_tx_id` named, for the return's whole amount: a cash sale was "refunded" to a stranger's charge id, and a free return (no sale) refunded a named id too | A card return refunds the original sale's own card tender's gateway transaction, capped at that tender's net amount less every cent earlier card returns of the sale took (409 `exceeds_card_tender` past it). A client `gateway_tx_id` is a 400 naming the field; a sale with no card tender behind a gateway transaction is a 409 `card_tender` (refund it from the drawer), a sale paid with several card tenders a 409 `card_tender` (a return cannot pick one), and a return that names no sale a 409 `card_refund_needs_sale`: the money never leaves through an id the client chose. The refund row (`payment_refunds.gateway_refund_id`) names the tender's own gateway transaction |
| C2-5r2 | returns: a cash return pays out of an open drawer | The return read the register's open session before its transaction and never locked or rechecked it: a return racing the close committed into the counted session (the drawer went short of its posted Z report), and a return with no session open paid cash out of no drawer at all (`till_session_id` null) | Inside the transaction the register's open session is locked FOR SHARE (the close's FOR UPDATE waits on it, so a close that follows aggregates the payout) and a cash return is refused 409 `no_open_session` when none is open (or the session closed under the lock: 409 `session_closed`). A card or account return may live sessionless and attaches the open session when one is open |
| C2-5r2 | returns: a legacy unlinked return counts against the cap | Migration 105 could not link a pre contract return line to its sale line, so the quantity cap ignored it: a sale half returned before the contract could be returned in full again through the linked route | The cap counts the sale's unlinked (legacy) return lines by product beside the linked ones, so the goods bound the refunds whichever route brought them back |
| C2-5r2 | returns: naming the walk-in customer explicitly is still the walk-in | The walk-in refusal keyed on "no customer resolved", so a request naming the walk-in customer's id with `refund_method: account` created the open credit memo the refusal exists to prevent | The resolved customer is compared with the walk-in's id: naming it explicitly refuses 409 `walk_in_account` the same way |
| C2-5r2 | void and complete: one gateway movement per sale, and every movement recorded | Three concurrent voids of one card sale each passed the pre-transaction guards and each called the gateway, so one voided sale made three refunds (two only in orphan rows); a completion whose second card charge was declined left the first charge standing with nothing recorded; a void whose second card refund failed left the first refund made with nothing recorded | The void claims its card refunds through a committed refund intent (migration 105's pos_refund_intents, one live claim per sale and tender) in its own short transaction, rechecking every guard under the sale row lock, then calls the gateway outside every transaction (ADR 0005 section 11) and writes the result inside its money transaction: a second void, or a card return of the same tender, is refused 409 refund_in_flight by the claim's unique index before it reaches the terminal, so concurrent claims make exactly one refund (the losing contenders answer 409 and move no money). A tender whose charge is declined (or fails) makes the completion reverse every charge the tenders before it already took, answering 502 `charge_not_reversed` when a reversal fails too; a refund that fails mid-way leaves every refund already taken recorded in committed `pos.gateway_refund_orphaned` rows while the sale stands as it was |
| C2-5r3 | void and card return: the refund intent (behaviour, no route change) | The card return called the gateway before its transaction with nothing serializing it: three concurrent card returns of one unit refunded the card three times, a void racing a card return refunded twice, and the card tender's cap was bypassed by two returns in flight. The void's refund, moved inside the transaction by the previous round, held the sale row and the session's FOR SHARE lock across the network call, against ADR 0005 section 11 and 14.2 C2-5 | Every card refund (the void's and the card return's) is claimed first through a committed refund intent, unique per sale and tender while pending (migration 105's pos_refund_intents): the claim's own short transaction rechecks every guard under the sale row lock (the return: the quantity caps and the card tender cap; the void: the status, revision, returns and session guards), then the gateway is called outside every transaction, and the money transaction marks the claim done as it posts. A second void or card return of the same tender answers 409 refund_in_flight before the terminal; a gateway failure releases the claim having moved no money; a refund whose transaction refused leaves the claim standing and the orphaned refund row recording it, refusing the next refund of the tender until it is reconciled |
| C2-5r3 | returns: a legacy return read with its own sign | Migration 105 leaves the legacy pos_return_lines and pos_returns positive (it negates only while copying into credit memos), and the cap reads negated them, so a legacy return RAISED the caps: a sale partly returned before the contract could be returned for more than it sold, to the card included | Each source is read with its own sign (ABS): the legacy quantity, the refunded tax and the card refunds. The legacy quantity and cents bear on the product's first sale line by position, once, so a sale of several lines of one product loses headroom on one line only; and the legacy cents lower that line's remaining money, so the rounded shares of later linked returns can never pass what the sale took |
| C2-5r3 | returns: one request names one sale line once | A request that named the same sale line several times priced each duplicate its own rounded share against the same earlier total, so three namings of a 1649 line refunded 1650 | The second naming of a sale line is a 400 naming lines[i].line_id; the quantity belongs in one line |
| C2-5r3 | returns: fractional partial returns refund the sale exactly | The tax remainder capped but never completed (six half returns of a 1650 + 146 line refunded 144 of the 146 tax, two cents stayed owed), and each restock cost its own rounded share (the six restocks relieved 978 of cost of goods sold against the 975 the sale relieved) | The return that finishes every taxable line of the sale takes the rest of the tax exactly, and each restocking line and kit component costs and stocks the difference between its cumulative returned position before and after the return, so repeated fractional returns sum to the sale's own tax and relieved cost |
| C2-5r3 | complete: a refused completion reverses every charge | The reversal loop answered the first failed reversal and never attempted the later charges, leaving them charged behind a sale that never was | Every charge already taken is attempted (each recorded in its own reversal row); the first failure is the 502 charge_not_reversed answered |
| C2-5r3 | migration 105: the invoice line links survive a down then up | The down dropped both the sale's invoice_id link and the sale lines' invoice_line_id, so a down then up cycle left every sale completed under 105 unlinked: a linked return fell back to today's average cost and a null invoice_line_id | The up backfills each sale line's invoice line from the sale's invoice (by product and order within it, product sale lines to product invoice lines only, so a kit, component, charge or text line is never paired by position with a line of another type), and the down keeps the invoice_id link column (the invoices survive the down; 105 never created them), so the cycle restores the links; said in both files' notes |
| C2-5r4 | pos: `POST /api/v1/pos/refund-intents/{id}/resolve` (new route) | A refund intent whose act died between the claim and the money transaction (or whose terminal call answered no verdict) stayed PENDING with nothing able to tell a refund that moved from one that did not, and nothing able to resolve it; it refused every later void and card return of the tender | The guarded admin route of FROM-PRINCIPAL 41: the finance and owner roles only, behind the branch middleware, and a machine key refused in the route itself whether bound or not (403 `forbidden`; PR 83's key allowlist, not merged here, will list it REFUSED). The body is `outcome` (`refund` with the gateway's `refund_tx_id`, or `no_refund` with the `reconciliation_reference` finance filed) and a `reason`; a bare resolve, a half record and an unknown outcome are 400 naming the field, an unknown claim 404, a claim that is not PENDING 409 `intent_not_pending`. The claim's sale is read through the caller's branch context, so a caller outside the branch finds a missing row (404) on both legs; and a caller whose context carries no branch (multi_branch_enabled on, default_branch_required off, no header) has the sale's own branch held to the caller's grants through the branch guard serve passes the document and purchase order handlers, the same missing row (404, nothing moved) outside them, so the route acts only on the caller's branch, header or not. The record cannot contradict the claim's own: a no refund over a claim carrying the terminal's refund id is 409 `refund_recorded`, a refund id that differs from the recorded one 409 `refund_tx_id_conflict`. A recorded refund books through the act's own money transaction with every cap and lock and completes the claim DONE with the recorded id; a void's resolution books only the resolved tender's refund, and the sale's void completes when no claim of the sale is PENDING anymore, from whichever leg empties them (a booked refund or a no refund alike, under the same sale and claim locks), so the order the claims are resolved in never decides the end state, while a void whose every claim settles without a booked refund keeps the sale completable by a fresh void (a declined tender keeps its charge as unapplied credit owed); between one tender's booked refund and the last claim's resolution the sale stands completed with the booked cents owed (the refund reversed its application, so the invoice is open again for them and the customer's balance carries them) until the last claim resolves and the void completes, and the close refuses while a claim of the session is pending, so the interim never slips into a counted drawer; `no_refund` marks the claim FAILED and moves nothing else. Both legs write one `pos.refund_intent.resolved` audit row naming the person, the intent, the outcome and the reason, inside the leg's transaction, and a refused replay writes no orphan row. The claim row is locked inside the resolution and read right after the sale row it settles under, and every claim state change refuses 409 `intent_not_pending` when it settles no row, so of concurrent resolutions exactly one books and a same outcome loser is told the race, never a state refusal the winner's own completion caused. Migration 105 gains `pos_refund_intents.request` (a return claim's own request shape) and an index on `(state, created_at)` for the finder; the gateway's refund id is written onto the claim in its own committed write the moment the terminal answers |
| C2-5r4 | void, card return, every other refund method and till close: the intent table is the one authority (behaviour, no route change) | A card return whose claim came after a committed void asked the terminal for a second refund of the same charge; a cash or account return paid goods a pending card claim had already paid at the terminal; the card return's gateway amount could differ from the booked amount by the remainder; a card void parked at the terminal let the close commit and was then refused `session_closed` after the card was refunded; every gateway error, a timeout included, freed the tender | Every claim (void and card return) is refused under the sale row lock, before the terminal, when a void intent of the tender is DONE (409 `tender_refunded`), when another claim of the tender stands (409 `refund_in_flight`), when it passes the tender less the booked card refunds (409 `exceeds_card_tender`), or when the sale is no longer completed (409 `invalid_state_transition`). A return refunded by any method but the card's own is 409 `refund_in_flight` while any intent of the sale is PENDING, and 409 `void_refund_recorded` while the sale's void has a booked tender refund (its goods were paid back at the terminal); the till close is 409 `refund_in_flight` while any intent of the session's sales is PENDING, naming the claims and the resolve route, and the till report's `expected_by_method` CARD figure subtracts the card refunds booked against the session's still completed sales' card tenders (the void refunds resolutions book, written straight against the tender's payment; a return's refund goes through its credit memo and never joins), dropping with the tender when the void completes, the same net the CASH figure carries for the session's returns' cash refunds. The card return restates its remainders and totals inside the claim, so the terminal is asked for the figure the lock sees, and the money transaction refuses (`refund_amount_changed`, the intent staying PENDING beside the orphan row that records the gateway's figure) when its restated total differs from the amount the terminal took, so the books never record a cent less than the terminal moved. A gateway error with no verdict leaves the intent PENDING with a `pos.refund_outcome_unknown` audit row (the intent id, outcome unknown); only the terminal's explicit DECLINED releases the claim (a PENDING or ERROR answer stands the same way). The gateway interface carries no idempotency key parameter, so none is passed |
| C2-5r4 | returns: a partly legacy line completes (behaviour, no route change) | A line a legacy return partly brought back never counted as complete: the linked return that brought the rest took its rounded share, so the remainder and the tax left cents owed to the customer | The legacy quantity the product's bearer line carries counts in the line's returned quantity and in the test that every taxable line is finished, so the return that brings the rest takes the line's whole remaining money and the sale's whole remaining tax |
| C2-5r4 | migration 105: the down refuses over settled sales' contract era lines and standing claims, and the backfill pairs product lines only (operational, no route) | The down deleted the kit, component, charge and text lines of completed sales along with the rest, losing history the invoice and the returns read; it dropped the refund intents table with any PENDING rows, silently releasing their tenders; and the up's backfill paired sale lines and invoice lines of one product by position whatever their type, so a kit line took a product invoice line's link and the product line was left unlinked | The down refuses with an exception naming the count while any KIT, COMPONENT, CHARGE or TEXT line belongs to a completed or voided sale and changes nothing (the lines of a sale that is neither are still deleted), and refuses while any refund claim is still PENDING (dropping the table would release its tender); the backfill numbers and matches PRODUCT sale lines to PRODUCT invoice lines only, on both sides. Both are tests on a scratch database |
| C2-5r8 | inventory: `POST /api/v1/inventory/adjust`, `POST /api/v1/inventory/transfer` and the purchase order receive's stock write (behaviour, no route change) | A request whose context carried a branch (the multi branch setting on with the header, or a branch bound key) that named a location acted on the branch's row with the most quantity instead: a cycle count of one yard overwrote another yard's units, a receipt landed in the largest yard, and a transfer moved the wrong rows | A named location reads exactly that location's row, refused with 403 on the stock routes (`ErrLocationOutsideBranch` in process, so the receive path is held too) when the row sits in a branch the caller does not carry, never substituted; a first receipt at a yard of the caller's branch still creates its row; only a request naming no location lets the branch rule pick a row, and it takes the branch's first row by creation, a stated rule, never the largest quantity silently; an unbound request keeps the legacy row with no location. The purchase order receive writes through the same read and is pinned beside the routes |
| C2-5r8 | pos: `POST /api/v1/pos/refund-intents/{id}/resolve`, the till close, void and card return claim rules (behaviour, no route change) | A no refund resolution could settle a claim whose own act's terminal refund call was still on the wire, freeing the tender; the terminal then refunded it, a later card return refunded the same tender again, and the drawer could close over the open call | Every claim carries an in flight lease (migration 109's `answer_due_at`, the `refundAnswerLease` constant) and an answer record (`answered_at`, `answer_state`): the terminal call is bounded, every path it can take stamps the answer (the refund id's committed write, the decline release, the no verdict record, and an abandon stamp for a tender whose call a refusal never reached), and the resolve refuses 409 `refund_in_flight` on an unanswered claim inside its lease under the claim's row lock; once the lease expires with no answer at all the claim is marked UNKNOWN and settles as recorded. The terminal's refund id is written onto the claim whatever state it is in, a recorded refund that never booked holds the tender against every later act (409 `tender_refunded`), and the refund booked must be the terminal's own id (`refund_tx_id_conflict`), so a late answer behind an early release can never be paid twice; the close keeps refusing while any claim of the session stands, live or stranded |
| C2-5r8 | payments: `POST /api/v1/payments`, `POST /api/v1/payments/{id}/applications`, the credit memo apply, the write off and the deposit paths (behaviour at a seam the counter owns) | An application could land on the interim invoice of a split void (its open booked cents), leaving a live application the void's completion could not reverse: the last claim then answered 409 `has_applications` on every attempt, the drawer could not close, and an absorbed walk-in credit could no longer be paid back to its card | While the invoice's sale has a standing void claim, every application path is refused with 409 `void_claim_pending` naming the claims (the AR core asks the counter through a guard serve wires), so the void always completes and a credit that might have been absorbed stays unapplied on its own payment; the declined tender's credit limit the ADR stated then was round 7's fungibility argument, replaced by round 9's held credit rule (the C2-5r9 rows below) |
| C2-5r8 | till: the CARD figure of `expected_by_method` (behaviour, no route change) | The CARD figure subtracted only the card refunds booked against the tenders' payments (a void's resolutions); a card return's refund rides its credit memo, so the figure disagreed with the books by every card return of the session | The figure also subtracts the session's card return refunds through their credit memos, the same join the CASH figure always used for its cash returns |
| C2-5r8 | till: `GET /api/v1/pos/till/{id}/zreport`, `GET /api/v1/pos/zreports` | The counter's first cut onto the wire contract changed the Z report's `payload` from a JSON object to a base64 encoded string, unnamed in this file: any reader of the frozen report had to decode it before parsing it | The `payload` is a JSON object again (the fragment declares `PosTillReport`, as before the cut) and the characterization golden stops masking the field, so the frozen report's figures are pinned |
| C2-5r8 | migration 109 `refund_intent_in_flight_lease` (operational, no route; numbered 109 from round 9 so PR 83's 107 and 108 stay free and the two branches merge in either order) | `pos_refund_intents` carried no record of its own terminal call | Three columns: `answer_due_at` (the in flight lease, defaulted for existing rows so their lease expires from the migration on), `answered_at` (stamped by every path that ends the claim's call) and `answer_state` (`IN_FLIGHT`, `ANSWERED`, `UNKNOWN`). The down drops the three columns; the up writes no data beyond them and applies again after it |
| C2-5r9 | payments: `POST /api/v1/payments/{id}/applications`, a receipt's own applications and the deposit paths (behaviour at a seam the counter owns) | A declined void tender's credit sat as unapplied cash on the tender's payment, and the apply route answered 200 for it: on the shared walk-in customer a later walk-in invoice absorbed an earlier walk-in's declined card credit (the fourteenth review's Area 5 shape), and the books then owed the wrong card holder | FROM-PRINCIPAL 60a: the credit is held to its own tender. The AR core asks the counter through a held credit guard serve wires (the origin read from `pos_tenders.payment_id`, the voided sale and the claim the void released), and while the tender's payment still carries the credit unapplied, every path that applies a payment's unapplied cash (all through the AR core's `applyLocked`: the apply route, a receipt's own applications, the deposit paths) refuses 409 `tender_credit_held` naming the origin, the void's sale and its claim, and nothing is written. The refusal precedes the invoice's own hold, so a credit bound for a held interim invoice is refused by its payment first |

Keyed on the void's own released claim: only a FAILED void claim of a voided sale whose tender's payment still carries the credit unapplied is paid back (any other claim 409 `intent_not_failed` or 409 `no_held_credit`, a claim of a sale whose void has not completed 409 `no_held_credit`, an already paid back credit 409 `no_held_credit`), and every guard runs before the terminal is asked, so a refusal never moves money at the terminal. The terminal call runs under the claim discipline every terminal refund runs under: migration 110 widens `pos_refund_intents.act` to `payback`, the payback opens its own live claim before the call (a concurrent payback 409 `refund_in_flight` through the unique live (sale, tender) index, the in flight lease holding the resolve route and the drawer close while the call is on the wire), the terminal's refund id lands on the claim in its own committed write the moment it answers, and the money transaction books the credit out through the AR core's refund (`RefundPayment`: the credit's unapplied cash falls to zero, the cash account moves by the refund) completing the claim DONE with one `pos.refund_intent.paid_back` audit row in the resolve route's shape (the person, the void's claim, the outcome, the reason; the payback's own claim rides beside it). Exactly one payback per tender |

| C5-1d | migration 104 `delivery_wire_contract` (operational, no route) | `vehicles`, `drivers`, `delivery_routes` and `deliveries` had nullable `created_at` and `updated_at` (the keyset lists order on `created_at` and every scan reads `updated_at` into a non-nullable value, so one NULL failed the whole list) and no `revision`; a legacy row whose `delivery_routes.status` or `deliveries.status` was never set left the reader scanning a NULL into a non-null string field and the request answering 500 | For each table, `created_at` NULL is filled with `NOW()`, `updated_at` NULL from the filled `created_at`, both set NOT NULL; `delivery_routes.status` NULL is filled with `'DRAFT'` and `deliveries.status` NULL with `'PENDING'` (the values the model and the legacy scans treat as the start state for a fresh row), both set NOT NULL; `revision BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 1` on all four (the DEFAULT serves the raw writers: the seed and the frozen integration seam, whose rows carry one too); keyset indexes `vehicles (created_at DESC, id DESC)`, `drivers (created_at DESC, id DESC)`, `delivery_routes (scheduled_date DESC, id DESC)` and `deliveries (route_id, stop_sequence, id)`. The down drops the indexes and the revision columns and restores the nullable shape; the filled timestamps and the filled statuses stay filled. Fix round 2 (PR 70 review round 4 P3-2 and round 3's left P3): the up file rewrote every legacy lowercase or stored non-vocabulary status to the UPPERCASE form the model and scans carry, and a stored value outside the known vocabulary mapped to `'DRAFT'` (route) or `'PENDING'` (stop). Fix round 3 (PR 70 review round 5 P2-1, lead decision on method): the up file now normalises spelling with `UPPER(BTRIM(status))` and maps the known synonyms the legacy vocabulary used explicitly (lowercase or spaced forms of a known value, `IN_PROGRESS` to `IN_TRANSIT`, `COMPLETE` to `COMPLETED`, `CANCELED` to `CANCELLED` for routes; trimmed and lowercase known values for stops). Any stored value still outside the vocabulary is sent to a TERMINAL non billing state of its table: a route becomes `'CANCELLED'` and a stop becomes `'FAILED'` (the values `TransitionRoute` and `TransitionStop` can never dispatch, deliver, fulfil or bill from). The mapping never sends a rewritten row to `'DRAFT'` or `'PENDING'`; both are live states and a legacy row of unknown spelling could have been a finished route or a delivered stop. Each rewrite is named in a `RAISE NOTICE` (the table, the id, the old value, the new value) so an operator has the audit trail. The mapping refuses to fail the migration, so an unattended up stays applyable on a polluted legacy database. A lowercase stored stop `'delivered'` lands as `'DELIVERED'`; an unknown stored route `'weird_legacy'` lands as `'CANCELLED'`; the read sees the storage, never a model-text fallback. Stated fallback for synonyms of route states a stop delivered as (PR 70 follow-ups from review round 7 N2): a stop stored as `COMPLETE`, `COMPLETED` or `IN_PROGRESS` (foreign spellings of route states that the stop vocabulary has no home for) is rewritten to `'FAILED'` with a `RAISE NOTICE` naming the id, the stored value and the new value; the migration is never edited (it is merged and may be applied), so an operator who reads a delivered stop as failed re marks the stop through the wire after 104 |

Forced by this module's wire change; no production file in `internal/order` is touched |
