| SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN | the whole `/api/v1` surface a machine key can reach (345 census routes, method plus pattern), held by one allowlist, `core/pkg/middleware/keybranch_allow.go` | ADR 0007 section 5.5 pinned a branch bound machine key to its branch, but only the branch middleware and the payload guard honoured the pin: a scope checked key passes the role guards on scope alone, and the fix that walled each route the sweep found was opt in, so every route a round missed failed open. Rounds 3 and 4 proved it by probe: the market index refresh preview returned other branches' customers and exposure; the customer scoped pricing and tax reads answered about branch B; a pricing rule naming a branch B customer's job set that customer's price with no audit row; dealer wide pricing writes (a rule naming neither customer nor job, a tier category rule) passed; and the GL, AP and bank reconciliation writes, the staff roster and the unfiltered pricing lists were open | Deny by default. One allowlist classifies every machine key reachable route as exactly one of WALLED (183, behind the branch middleware), CONFINED (28, held by the key branch wall or the customer branch wall, each entry naming its test), STATED LIMIT (69, reachable unconfined by design, each entry with its one line reason) or REFUSED (65, answered 403 `forbidden` with the `key.branch_refused` row). The one runtime check is `KeyBranchRouteGate`, mounted once in serve's chain inside auth: it resolves the request's registered pattern through the serve mux and refuses a bound key every route the table does not admit, a route the router resolves to no pattern included, so a missed or new route fails closed. A census test (untagged, no database) fails when a machine key reachable census route is unclassified or a table entry is stale. The confinements are completed: `job_id` resolves to its projects row's customer beside `customer_id` (both are checked), the customer scoped reads that name a customer in a path, query or body are confined like the writes (the exemption read, the category rule list by `customer_id` with the unfiltered list refused, the rule audit, resolve, calculate, the tax preview), and a write that would be dealer wide is refused: a pricing rule naming neither a customer nor a job, a category rule or bulk element targeting a tier, and a bulk delete naming a tier row. REFUSED outright beside the round 3 list: the GL, AP and bank reconciliation writes (reads already refused), the staff roster and its module grants, the index refresh preview, `GET /pricing/rules`, `GET /pricing/matrix` and the unfiltered category rule list. Stated limits stay reachable with their reasons in the table, and ADR 0007 section 5.5 names them and advises which scopes not to mint on a bound key. Users and unbound keys keep exactly their reach on every route |
| SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN-3 | the branch bound key's classes and walls, fix round 3 (the same 345 census routes as SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN) | Rounds 5 and 6 proved by probe that three classes overstated: a tier category rule that also named a customer passed the customer wall and priced every branch's customers of the tier (201, no audit row); the POS till, Z report, return and line routes and the account summary, AR statement and reconciliation loaded their row by id with no branch predicate, so a key bound to A closed B's till (posting its over/short and freezing its Z report), added a line to B's transaction that stayed written behind the 500, and read B's till report, Z reports, returns, account, statement and reconciliation; `GET /pricing/calculate` confined its `customer_id` but not its `job_id`; a tax preview naming only B's `ship_to_id` passed; and the 18 dealer wide catalogue writes labelled WALLED (products, PIM, charge codes, payment terms, the reorder refresh) and every other stated limit write were reachable by a bound key | The category rule wall, handler and table now keep one invariant (an account rule carries no tier, a tier rule no customer), and migration 107 puts it on the table checking existing rows first: a row that breaks it is reported by a NOTICE naming the count and ids, never rewritten, and the constraint goes on NOT VALID when one exists, validated on add otherwise. The pos repositories carry the branch predicate the transaction reads already carried (`till_sessions.branch_id`, `till_z_reports.branch_id`, `pos_returns.branch_id`, the line writes through their transaction's branch, the insert never happening on a foreign transaction), the account summary, subledger and statement mount the customer branch wall on the path customer, and the AR reconciliation is refused outright (its ledger half carries no branch fact). The calculation resolves `job_id` to `projects.customer_id` like the write; a tax preview naming a `ship_to_id` is confined through that ship to's customer. The classes are split per the principal's rulings: a stated limit is a read, never a write (WALLED 145, CONFINED 31, STATED LIMIT 51, REFUSED 118), so every dealer wide write (the catalogue and PIM writes above, the settings, module and app switches, the match tolerances, vendors, EDI partners and the catalog import, units, pricing categories, rebates, millwork options, governance rfcs, the index metadata) is refused with the `key.branch_refused` row, the driver routes are refused reads included (personal data, no branch column), and the vehicle reads stay stated limits. Every WALLED entry names the column or join its module filters on and a twin sweep drives every WALLED id or list route with the other branch's row (refused, or the missing row answer, nothing written). A shared customer may be read by a bound key but its customer scoped pricing rules and tax exemptions are refused unless the pin holds the customer alone, the refusal naming the other branch; the grant, revoke and home branch writes record their audit rows with the key as the actor; the parsing upload and vision scan stay stated limits and each call records its AI spend audit row with the key that spent it |
| SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN-4 | the branch bound key's walls, fix round 4 (the same 345 census routes: WALLED 145, CONFINED 31, STATED LIMIT 51, REFUSED 118; no route added or removed) | Rounds 8 and 9 probed through the real wiring and found: a key bound to A could post a payment (with its GL entry), a credit memo, an order (taking the other customer's tax exemption) or a quote for a customer held only by branch B, move a quote or a draft order onto it, and promote a draft whose payload named it; an order carried another customer's ship to (its address in the answer) or contact, for every caller; a legacy tier rule that also named an A customer was deleted by an A key, singly and in bulk; a customer shared with B could have its tier, credit limit, terms, currency, active flag, escalation policy and contacts changed by the A key; the order exposure gate and override looked up the source quote by order id alone; and the twin sweep sent bodies that failed validation, counted whole tables other packages move, and left about 25 walled routes undriven | The customer a body names is held to the pin on POST `/payments`, `/payments/card`, `/credit-memos` (and PUT), `/orders` and `/quotes` (and their PUTs) by the key branch wall composed in the `branchWall` mount methods, and on the draft promotions by the customer in the draft's stored payload (`wall.draftGuard`); a refused call is 403 naming `customer_id` with the `key.branch_refused` row and nothing written. An order create or update refuses a `ship_to_id` or `ordered_by_contact_id` its customer does not own (400 naming the field, for every caller). Any category rule row whose target is not an account rule is dealer wide in the row and bulk walls, and the rule audit read now uses the plain customer wall (a shared customer's rule audit is readable, PD-4). The order exposure gate and override read the order under the branch wall first. The customer service holds the shared customer rule (principal rulings 43 to 45, deny by default): on a customer shared with another branch a bound key changes only `email` and `phone`; every other header field, the salesperson and the escalation policy are refused in the PD-4 words naming the other branch; ship-tos and contacts of a shared customer may be added but not changed or deleted; a dealer wide key changes them with a `customer.shared_restricted_changed` audit row naming the key and the before and after values (the field by field table is in ADR 0007 section 5.5). The "also held by branch" refusal names the lowest numbered other branch, never the pin. The parsing upload is audited before the extractor runs. Tests: the twin sweep is rebuilt on bodies that pass validation with an own twin control per write, measures changes by the twins' ids, and a coverage test fails by name for any WALLED route no bound key leg drives; migration 107, the bulk category delete on a shared customer, the AI and grant audit deltas and serve's own audit wiring are tested. The fixture builds the order service as serve does and removes the rows and keys it makes. Users and unbound keys keep their reach, apart from the order ship to and contact ownership, which holds for them too |
| SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN-5 | the branch bound key's walls, fix round 5 (the same 345 census routes: WALLED 145, CONFINED 31, STATED LIMIT 51, REFUSED 118; no route added or removed) | Rounds 10 and 11 probed through the real wiring and found: the offline POS sync replayed a sale (and its ACCOUNT tender's invoice) on a customer held only by branch B; an order or a quote took a `job_id` of B's project, and the order was priced by B's job rule, for every caller; a bound key converted a quote at branch A whose customer was held only by B, the order taking B's tax exemption; a bound key's ship-to add with `is_default: true` cleared the shared customer's default ship to; a CRM activity of A's customer named B's contact, for every caller; a shared customer's restricted fields were open to every user and every unbound key; a legacy tier row's PUT answered 500 for the unbound key and the user; the intent route's wall refused a bound key for a body field the route does not accept; and the credit memo PUT wall, the PUT body's salesperson, the third branch's naming order, the service level refusal row's method and path, the escalation audit's signature date and the coverage test's control legs were untested | The offline sync is walled through the customer each replayed sale names (`wall.pos`, items[].customer_id; no pos code touched). The order and quote creates and updates hold their body's `job_id` through `projects.customer_id` (`bodyDocumentCustomer`), the conversion rechecks the quote's stored customer under the customer wall (`quoteRowCustomerOf`), and for every caller the order build, the conversion's build and the quote's priceDraft refuse a `job_id` its customer does not own (400 naming `job_id`, the credit memo's words); a CRM activity refuses a `contact_id` its customer does not own the same way. A ship-to add naming `is_default: true` is refused on a shared customer like a change (checkSharedChild), so the shared default is never cleared by an add. The principal's item 48 ruling: a shared customer's restricted fields change only with the finance or owner role (a user; sales and admin are refused in the same words naming a branch, no claims fails closed) or an unbound key carrying the dedicated `customers:restricted` scope (in the grant grammar, required by no route so no default grant carries it, minted only by an owner with the `key.created` row naming it; every other unbound key refused with the same words, a bound key refused even with it); the `customer.shared_restricted_changed` audit row stays, and a user's change writes none; the customer routes' guard admits finance. An update of a legacy single target row answers a 409 naming the invariant for every caller instead of the 500. The intent create leaves the body customer wall: its own 400 unknown field answer serves every caller. The escalation audit's before and after values carry `agreement_signed_at` beside the reference, a service level refusal row names the method and path `RecordRequest` noted, the shared customer is held by a third branch so every also held by answer names the lowest numbered other branch on each of its three queries, and the walled route coverage counts only the drivers' explicit twin marks, so an A control alone covers nothing. The pre existing draft order promote 500 (round 11 P3-3) is left for a later item. The field by field table is in ADR 0007 section 5.5 |
| SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN-6 | the branch bound key's walls, fix round 6 (the same 345 census routes: WALLED 145, CONFINED 31, STATED LIMIT 51, REFUSED 118; no route added or removed) | Round 13 and the principal's item 54 rulings found: a bound key's pos sync on a register whose location row names no branch wrote an open transaction at the default branch, because the register's branch was read only through `locations` and not from `pos_registers.branch_id` (the till open and the return derived it the same way); the refusal of a user without the finance or owner role, or of an unbound key without `customers:restricted`, on a shared customer's restricted fields used other words than the bound key's and wrote no row; the customer routes admitted finance on every route; and a bound key changed and deleted a CRM activity of a shared customer | A register's branch is `COALESCE(l.branch_id, r.branch_id)` in the transaction create, the till open and the return branch lookup, so the pin check always runs. The caller refusals use the bound key's words and write a `customer.shared_restricted_refused` row (actor, fields, branch, method, path). Finance reaches only the header write, the salesperson and the escalation policy; every other customer route is back to `admin`, `owner`, `sales`. On a shared customer a bound key adds an activity and never changes or deletes one (403 in the contact rule's words, with its `key.branch_refused` row). New tests: the register branch legs, the stored job recheck on the conversion, the claims-less mint refusal and a bound key that truly carries the scope |
| SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN-POS | pos: `POST /api/v1/pos/till/open` (and the till session reads that return it), goldens `pos`, `pos_transactions`, `pos_returns`, `pos_till_close` | A till session opened on a register whose location names no branch was stamped with no branch (`branch_id` absent from the session) | The session carries the register's branch (`COALESCE(l.branch_id, r.branch_id)`), so `branch_id` appears in the till session body; the seed also moves the seeded `REG-01` onto Kelowna (an `ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE` on `location_id` and `branch_id` alone that fires only while the row sits exactly as migrations 027 and 066 left it: the placeholder location AND the branch equal to `default_branch_id` as the seed read it before changing anything; updates `location_id` and `branch_id` only, so it keeps the register's name and never re-points a register an operator moved on its own), so a seeded register belongs to the branch the seed makes the default, which leaves every sale and return amount in the goldens unchanged. No route, import or seed other than this one writes `system_settings.default_branch_id`, so the two states that re-fire the upsert need a hand edit: an operator who hand-sets `default_branch_id` to their branch and leaves REG-01 on the placeholder at that branch (the row is then byte-for-byte the migrations' shape; a demo seed under `DEMO_SEED` moves it, r21 P3-1), or a previous run that found `default_branch_id` changed or absent rewrote it to Kelowna so a later run moves REG-01's placeholder location (the branch is unchanged; an operator who re-pointed REG-01 by its branch to another branch is never moved, r20 P3-1) |
| SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN-7 | the branch bound key's walls, fix round 7 (the same 345 census routes: WALLED 145, CONFINED 31, STATED LIMIT 51, REFUSED 118; no route added or removed) | Round 15 and the principal's item 57 rulings found: the till, Z report and return reads' branch predicate hid every row written before the column was stamped, so a drawer open at upgrade read as no drawer (close 409, open 409) and the legacy Z reports and returns vanished from every caller with a branch context; a user's register branch refusal on the sale answered 500; the till open and a return stamped the register's branch whatever the caller's branch context named; a bound key's sync logged its batch under another branch's register; the CONTRACT-CHANGES file held two rows with the id `SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN-6`; finance could not read the customer it may change; and a sales user read "a branch bound key may act only on its own branch" on a shared customer's restricted fields | Migration 108 backfills `branch_id` on `till_sessions`, `till_z_reports` and `pos_returns` from each row's own register (`COALESCE(the register's location's branch, pos_registers.branch_id)`), touching only rows whose branch is NULL, counting what it set per table in a NOTICE, reporting by count and first ids any row whose register names no branch and leaving it as it is, moving no money and deleting nothing; its down leaves the data. A register branch refusal is a typed error answered 403 with its words on the sale, the till open, the return and the whole sync batch (the batch register is held to the caller's branch context before any item runs and before the `pos_sync_log` row is written). Finance may read `GET /customers/{id}` and `GET /customers/{id}/escalation-policy` (reads only, the reads its writes need for `If-Match`; every other customer, contact, ship-to and activity route keeps the roles it had). The shared customer refusals carry one clause naming the other branch behind a caller specific lead in: "this key may act only on its own branch" for a bound key, "this key may not change a shared customer's restricted fields" for an unbound key without the scope, "this role may not change a shared customer's restricted fields" for a user without the finance or owner role (ADR 0007 section 5.5 quotes them) |
| SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN-8 | the branch bound key's walls, fix round 8 (the same 345 census routes: WALLED 145, CONFINED 31, STATED LIMIT 51, REFUSED 118; no route added or removed) | Rounds 16 and 17 found: the seed's REG-01 upsert moved a register migration 027 created that an operator later re-pointed, because its `DO UPDATE` was unconditional (item 57's condition reads migration 027 as another seed); the CONTRACT-CHANGES file held two rows with the id `SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN-7`, the pos row and round 7's; `GET /pos/till/{id}/report` answered 500 for a session the caller's branch context hides, where the other by id reads answer 404; the exclusive walls still led the shared customer refusal with "a branch bound key" in words ADR 0007 section 5.5 does not quote; and a sync batch naming a register that does not exist ran its items and was logged under that register | The seed's upsert updates only while REG-01 still sits on migration 027's placeholder location, so a re-seed never moves a register an operator re-pointed (nor renames one; the register a person made is untouched as before). Every row of this PR carries a unique id: the pos row its own (`SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN-POS`), each round's rows under their own id. The hidden till session reads as missing (404, the Z report and return words; the spec entry updated). The exclusive walls' refusal carries the quoted lead in "this key may act only on its own branch", the customer service's bound key words, so every shared customer refusal a bound key meets carries words the ADR quotes. A sync batch whose `register_id` names no register is refused 400, a typed error, before any item runs and before the `pos_sync_log` row, which never names an unknown register (spec entry, allowlist notes and ADR 0007's sync passage updated) |
| SEC-BOUND-KEY-PIN-9 | the branch bound key's walls, fix round 9 (the same 345 census routes: WALLED 145, CONFINED 31, STATED LIMIT 51, REFUSED 118; no route added or removed) | Rounds 18 and 19 found: while REG-01 still sat on migration 027's placeholder location, which names no locations row, the register's branch came from pos_registers.branch_id alone, so an operator who re-pointed it by its branch alone had moved it as completely as by its location, and the next demo re-seed moved it back (item 59's "the operator's row is never moved"); a sync batch with an empty register_id still ran its items and was logged under a register nobody made (the log row carried an empty register_id); and the PR's exclusive wall test left the refusal's audit row unasserted (its checks were placeholders). Round 18 also asked whether the unknown batch register 400, which answers for every caller, is the right class | The seed's upsert fires only on the row exactly as migrations 027 and 066 left it: the placeholder location AND the branch 066 gave it, the `default_branch_id` the seed reads before it changes anything (a database whose default an operator changed keeps REG-01 where it is, the safe side). The `DO UPDATE` SET lists `location_id` and `branch_id` only, never `name` or `is_active`, and the conflict key is the register id, so a re-seed moves exactly the register the migrations created, keeps its name, leaves every other register untouched, and a rerun against that row is a no-op. Two hand-built states still re-fire the upsert and need naming; neither has a route. First, an operator who hand-sets `system_settings.default_branch_id` to their own branch and leaves REG-01 on the placeholder at that branch produces a row byte-for-byte the migrations' shape; under `DEMO_SEED` the seed moves it (r21 P3-1). Second, after a run that found the setting changed or absent, the seed rewrites it to Kelowna's id, so a later run moves REG-01's placeholder location to a row that exists, never an operator's re-pointed branch (r20 P3-1). A sync batch whose `register_id` is empty or names no register is refused 400 for every caller (a register that does not exist is a bad request for anyone, not only a bound key; stated here as round 18 P3-2 asked), before any item runs and before the `pos_sync_log` row; the exclusive wall test asserts the pinned key's `key.branch_refused` row, its method, path and pin branch, beside the refusal words. The repeated ids that already repeat on refactor/v1 (PR 99's C4-1b rows among them, 22 ids in all) predate this PR and are left alone: a whole file pass on citable ids is the principal's hygiene work, not this PR's |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/invoices/{id}/transitions` to approved, on a pending bill carrying a paid amount (review fix) | A legacy pending bill with `amount_paid <> 0` (an unknown status migration 106 made PENDING, or a hand edited row) approved whole: the entry credited `2010` with the entire total while `amount_open` stood at the migration's clamp (zero on a bill paid past its total, the rest on a partly paid one), so `2010` held money no bill owed and no route cleared it | The approve refuses it with a 409 `invalid_state_transition` and the `has_paid_amount` blocker naming the paid cents; nothing is written and the row is left for the operator to mend by hand (new data cannot reach the shape: the pay applies only to an approved or partial bill) |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/invoices/{id}/transitions` to voided, the no reversal arm (review fix) | The arm was keyed on the bill's total: an approved bill with a nonzero total and no `gl_entry_id` was refused `entry_not_linked` (even a legacy bill whose entry never posted or was already reversed, holding no credit in `2010`), while a legacy bill hand edited to a zero total after its approve voided with nothing reversed, stranding its posted credit in `2010` under a VOIDED bill | The arm is keyed on the ledger: a bill that holds a posted, unreversed `VENDOR_INVOICE` entry it is not linked to (a legacy bill whose posting was swallowed, one holding the removed sync's one leg credit, or one whose total was hand edited after its approve, whatever its total says now) is refused `entry_not_linked` and keeps its status; a bill with no live entry (one that prices to zero, or a legacy bill whose entry never posted or was already reversed) holds no credit and voids with nothing to reverse; a linked live entry still voids reversed whole |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/invoices` currency default (review fix, PR 89 r1 P2-1) | `Parse` set an omitted currency to `USD` before the service saw it, so the `currency.default` setting was never read on the route (only an in-process caller with an empty currency reached it) | `Parse` leaves an omitted currency empty and the create's `resolve` reads the `currency.default` setting for it (`USD` where the setting is absent), on the route as in process; a currency the request names is still validated as an ISO 4217 code of three capital letters |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/invoices/{id}/transitions` to voided, a reversal of any status (review fix, PR 99 r2 P1-1) | The void's live entry predicate counted a reversal row of any status as an undo, but the trial balance sums POSTED entries alone, so a bill whose entry's reversal was voided (the GL void route voids any posted entry, a reversal included) still held its credit live in `2010` while the predicate read the bill as dead: the unlinked bill voided 200 with nothing reversed and the credit stood stranded under a VOIDED bill | A reversal counts only while it is POSTED: the predicate matches the trial balance, and such a bill is refused `entry_not_linked` with nothing written, its credit staying reachable under a bill that still owes it (migration 106's link predicate reads a reversal of any status as an undo too, which leaves such a bill unlinked: the safer side, since the entry's one reversal row is taken and the void could not reverse it anyway) |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/invoices/{id}/transitions` to voided, the linked arm (review fix, PR 99 r2 P2-1) | With `gl_entry_id` set the void called `gl.PostReversal` with no live check, so a bill whose linked entry the GL routes had already reversed or voided answered 500 `internal_error` (the already reversed and the not POSTED refusals are mapped nowhere), contrary to the arm's own rule that a bill holding no credit voids with nothing to reverse | The linked arm is keyed on the ledger: a linked entry that is not live (not POSTED, or a POSTED reversal points at it, or it was deleted by hand) holds no credit and the bill voids with nothing to reverse; a linked entry that is live while a not POSTED reversal row points at it is refused with the 409 `reversal_not_posted` blocker and nothing is written (its credit stands in `2010`, and the one reversal an entry may hold is already taken, so no new reversal can post); never a 500 |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/payments` entry currency (review fix, PR 99 r2 P2-2) | `PayVendor` posted its entry with no currency, so the entry took the `currency.default` setting's code whatever the bills carried: paying a bill entered under a since changed setting, or a named currency bill, posted the payment in the setting's currency and `2010` drifted per currency (the trial balance never sums across two) | The payment entry posts in the bills' currency, and a payment naming bills of more than one currency is refused with the 409 `currency_mismatch` blocker naming the bills that differ, nothing written; `2010` ties per currency after every act |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/invoices/{id}/transitions` to voided, a double posted bill (review fix, PR 99 r2 P3-1) | A bill holding a live `VENDOR_INVOICE` entry beside its linked one (the base read the status outside its transaction, so a double click could post it twice; only the newest is linked) voided 200 reversing the linked entry alone, stranding the duplicate's credit in `2010` under a VOIDED bill no route could reach | The void is refused with the 409 `entry_not_linked` blocker naming the other live entry by its number and id; nothing is written and the bill keeps its status until the duplicate is reversed |
| C4-1b | ap: a legacy pending bill carrying a paid amount (review note, PR 99 r1 P3-3) | Such a bill can be neither approved (the `has_paid_amount` refusal) nor voided (the `has_payments` refusal runs first), and no route edits `amount_paid` | Stated limit: the row is parked for the operator to mend by hand, as the blocker text says; a later repair item should give the operator a route (new data cannot reach the shape: the pay applies only to an approved or partial bill) |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/invoices/{id}/transitions` to voided, liveness over the whole reversal chain (review fix, PR 99 r4 P1-1) | Both liveness predicates (`HasPostedInvoiceEntry` for the unlinked arm, `LinkedEntryLedger` for the linked one) and the double posted check looked one level down the chain: a POSTED reversal pointing at the bill's entry read as dead even when that reversal's own reversal (posted through the public GL reverse route) had put the credit back, so the void answered 200 with nothing reversed and left the bill VOIDED with its full credit live in `2010`, linked or not (a regression this PR introduced: the base answered 500 or `entry_not_linked`, each writing nothing) | A bill's credit is live exactly when the POSTED entries of its VENDOR_INVOICE entry and that entry's whole reversal chain (walked down `reverses_entry_id` through entries of any status, summed on `2010` as the trial balance sums it) leave a net amount; the void, the double posted check and the approve all decide on that net. A live linked entry whose one reversal row is already taken (of any status, so no new reversal can post under the one reversal index) is refused with the 409 `reversal_not_posted` blocker naming the shape, nothing written |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/invoices/{id}/transitions` to approved, a bill already holding a live entry (review fix, PR 99 r4 P3-2) | A legacy PENDING bill whose posting ran before the status moved (the base's sync could post first; migration 106 links only APPROVED or PARTIAL, so the row carries its entry unlinked) approved whole into a second credit: `2010` held the total twice while the bill owed it once, and a whole payment later left the surplus under a PAID bill (predates the PR; the PR's void made the shape better, not worse) | The approve refuses it with a 409 `invalid_state_transition` and the `entry_not_linked` blocker naming the live entry by its number and id, nothing written, whatever depth the entry's reversal chain has reached (the chain lens above); a bill whose chain holds no credit approves into the one credit it owes |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/invoices/{id}/transitions` to voided racing a GL reverse (review fix, PR 99 r4 P3-1) | When an AP void and a GL reverse of the same linked entry raced, the loser's reversal insert met the one reversal index `ux_gl_entry_single_reversal` (a pre-check cannot see the uncommitted contender) and the duplicate key error passed up unmapped, so the AP void answered 500 `internal_error` on a money route the PR body says the `ErrAlreadyReversed` belt covers; nothing was written and the ledger held, so a retry voided cleanly | `gl.PostEntry` maps a unique violation on a reversal insert to `ErrAlreadyReversed`, which the void answers with the same 409 `reversal_not_posted` as the belt, nothing written: never a 500, exactly one reversal of the entry stands whichever side wins, and the bill voids or stays voidable |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/invoices/{id}/transitions` to voided, the reversal's currency (review fix, PR 99 r4 P3-3) | The void handed `gl.PostReversal` the bill's current currency, and the reversal took the currency it was handed, so a bill whose currency had been hand edited after its approve (no route changes it; legacy only) voided with the reversal posted in the bill's new currency: the entry's credit stood in one currency and its reversal in another, so `2010` tied in neither (the trial balance never sums across two) | `gl.PostReversal` posts in the original entry's own currency (`GetJournalEntry` reads it), the input's currency kept only as the fallback for an entry that carries none; both currencies tie at zero after the void |
| C4-1b | gl: `POST /api/v1/gl/journal-entries/{id}/reverse`, the reversal's currency (review fix, PR 99 r6 P2-1) | The route built its reversal with no currency, so the insert took the `currency.default` setting's code instead of the entry's: reversing a CAD entry under a USD setting posted the reversal in USD, and with the AP acts' chain liveness summing the two currencies together the AP void read the chain as dead (CAD 20 and USD -20 net to zero), answered 200, reversed nothing and left the bill VOIDED with its CAD credit live in the CAD ledger (a regression this PR introduced: on the base the same shape answered 500 with nothing written) | The reversal posts in the original entry's own currency (`GetJournalEntry` reads it), so both halves of one undo sit in one currency and `2010` ties per currency after the act |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/invoices/{id}/transitions`, the chain liveness net per currency (review fix, PR 99 r6 P2-1) | Both liveness reads (`HasPostedInvoiceEntry` and `LiveInvoiceEntryOtherThan` through `chainLive`, and `LinkedEntryLedger` with its own copy of the walk) summed `credit - debit` on `2010` across every currency in the chain at once, so a chain holding two currencies whose whole sums cancel read dead (the trial balance never sums across two, yet liveness did): the AP void and the legacy approve then answered 200 over an amount still standing in one of the two currencies, whatever way the chain had arisen (a legacy reversal posted in the wrong currency included) | The net is computed per currency and the chain is live exactly when any one currency nets nonzero: such a chain is refused (the void with the `reversal_not_posted` blocker on the linked arm, `entry_not_linked` unlinked and on the legacy approve), nothing written, and no mixed currency chain, however it arose, can read as dead |
| C4-1b | ap: `POST /api/v1/ap/invoices/{id}/transitions` to voided racing a GL void of the linked entry (review fix, PR 99 r6 P3-1) | When a GL void of the linked entry committed between the void's ledger read and the reversal's own read of the entry, `gl.PostReversal`'s plain refusal to reverse an entry that is no longer POSTED passed up unmapped, so the AP void answered 500 `internal_error` on a money route (nothing was written by the loser; sequentially the same shape voids 200 with nothing to reverse) | The refusal is the typed `gl.ErrNotPosted`, which the void maps beside `ErrAlreadyReversed` to the same 409 `reversal_not_posted`, nothing written: a fifty round race never answers 500 on either side, the loser's retry voids 200 with nothing to reverse, and every entry stays balanced |

A credit limit of 0 means no account charges at all: any `account` tender is refused 409 with the blocker `credit_hold`, before any card is charged and again under the credit lock, whatever unapplied cash the account holds. The PO check is repeated against the locked sale row. The lookup treats `%`, `_` and `\` in its query as text |

| M3 | `GET /api/v1/dealer-profile`, `PUT /api/v1/dealer-profile`, `GET /api/v1/dealer-profile/logo`, `PUT /api/v1/dealer-profile/logo` (new, migration 115) | No dealer identity existed: every printed form carried the fixed title GABLE LBM, and no logo, trading name, contact lines or remit to address could be set. | One dealer wide profile (legal and trading name, phone, email, website, remit to, logo) on the wire recipe. Read by admin, owner, sales, finance and purchasing; written by admin and owner only, under the revision precondition (428 without one, 409 `stale_revision`), each write one transaction with an audit row holding the before and after (the logo row holds size and digest, never the bytes). The logo is the raw body, PNG or JPEG, at most 524288 bytes, whose bytes must decode as the declared type (413, 415, 400 otherwise). A branch bound machine key is refused both writes (REFUSED); the reads are stated limits. A logo is also refused with a 400 when its decoded size is past 2000 pixels a side or 4 megapixels, when it is a 16 bit or interlaced PNG (the forms cannot embed those), when it does not decode whole, or when bytes follow the image's end (a JPEG's end marker; a payload hidden inside the JPEG's last scan is not caught there, so a JPEG upload is stored as its decoded image written again at a fixed quality, content type unchanged, and nothing carried inside or after its scan survives; a PNG is stored byte for byte); a stored logo past the size cap prints without the image. |
| M3 | `GET /api/v1/email-deliveries` (new, migration 115) | A send left no record: the log sender wrote a log line and nothing else. | Every customer or vendor facing send writes a row (recipient, subject, document type, id and number, status `sent` or `failed`, the sender's answer, any error, who sent it). The list is the ADR 0001 envelope newest first with the `document_type`, `status` and `document_id` filters and `include=total`, role gated admin, owner and finance, walled by the carried document's branch (a row of no branch is hidden from a walled caller). |

Roles admin, owner, sales, finance (a role refusal is a 403); a customer the caller may not see is a 404; a branch bound machine key reaches it only while the customer holds its pin (CONFINED). Wire error envelope. The customer wall is the customer read's (header, grants, customer_branches), not the primary branch alone: a customer the caller's branch shares prints and emails its statement. |

The message now goes through the configured sender (SMTP when `SMTP_HOST` is set, the log sender otherwise), with a subject naming the invoice number and the dealer and a file named for the number, and the outcome is written to `email_deliveries`. |

| M3 | process configuration: `SMTP_HOST`, `SMTP_PORT`, `SMTP_USERNAME`, `SMTP_PASSWORD`, `SMTP_FROM`, `SMTP_TLS` (operational) | Every email was a log line (`LogEmailService`); there was no sender configuration anywhere. | With `SMTP_HOST` set, serve and the worker send through an SMTP sender (STARTTLS by default, implicit TLS with `SMTP_TLS=tls`, clear text with `none` for a local catcher; optional PLAIN authentication, refused in clear text off loopback); a set host with no usable `SMTP_FROM`, an unknown `SMTP_TLS`, a bad port or a username without a password refuses to start. With no host the log sender stays and keeps disclosing itself through `DeliveryDescription()`, which the real sender does not implement. |

The run (admin and owner, the pricing rules' role wall) takes one to twelve distinct product ids, calls the configured flow in batches of three, and answers per product our walk in price, the competitor's match, our recomputed difference and position and a suggested price that keeps the margin floor (409 `not_configured` when unset). No golden changes. |

The same two causes (a check dated ahead now lands on the seed's day, so 196103 cents move from receivables to cash as of today; the restamp spreads each row's stamp from the seed's moment over its document's day) also move, in `clockwindow`: the thirty day `total_collected` 315919.09 to 77355.25, `today_revenue_change`, the revenue trend's day before yesterday 20202 to 1259336 and yesterday 10101 to 454006, and the trial balance (1010 Cash debit 31163382 to 31359485, 1020 receivables credit 31190099 to 31386202, 2200 Customer Deposits both sides, the balance sheet's Cash 31158382 to 31354485 and receivables 9181059 to 8984956); and in `gl_accounts_entries`: the two PAY-000013 payment entries leave the ahead dated position and about 125 entries' `created_at` and `updated_at` move to their documents' days (no amount, number or account moved). A payment received, an application, a credit memo application, a write off and a refund dated after the branch's business day are refused with a 400 naming the field. The statement dates an invoice by its invoice day, a credit memo by its memo day, a payment by its received day, an application by its applied day and a refund by its refunded day, and splits the opening balance, the period and the order on that day. The aging cursor of a customer grouping reads on the next page. |
| G1-FIX (item 130, N12, N13 round 1) | AR core acts `Reverse`, `VoidPayment`, `PostInvoice`, `VoidInvoice`, `PostCreditMemo`, `VoidCreditMemo`; `GET /api/v1/ar/customers/{id}/statement` (new fields `unapplied_payments`, `unapplied_cents`, `net_balance_cents` on each currency, golden `account`) | The core accepted a reversal, a void, an invoice day or a credit memo day after the branch's business day, so the aging counted an application as live while the invoice read unpaid. The subledger writes a row when cash is applied, never on receipt, so a customer with a deposit and an open invoice read the full invoice on the statement and the net on the aging. | Each of those acts is refused with a 400 naming `reversed_on`, `voided_on`, `invoice_date` or `memo_date` when its day has not come. The day is the act's own document's branch's day in that branch's zone; the aging's default and the dashboard use the default branch's zone, so a branch east of the default may hold a day that is today there and a day ahead of the default aging for some hours. The statement lists each currency's payments still unapplied as of its last day (number, received day, amount), their sum, and the net balance (closing balance less unapplied cash), which equals the aging's total on that day. |
| G1-FIX (item 130, N14, N16 round 1) | `GET /api/v1/documents/print/statement/{id}` document content; the desk's prints of the statement, the invoice, the pick ticket and the counter receipt (no route or wire shape change) | The statement document carried no aging, and a desk print opened a bare tab on the API's address, which carries no token. | The document draws the customer's aging as of the period's last day on the due date basis, over the whole account the closing balance covers (the buckets, unapplied cash and credits in their own column, and the total equal to the net balance), the payments still unapplied with their received days, and the net balance; a paid up account draws zeros; a job statement draws none. The desk fetches each document with the signed in token and shows it from a blob in a tab opened during the click. |
| G1-FIX (item 130, N13 round 2) | `GET /api/v1/ar/customers/{id}/statement`, `unapplied_payments`, `unapplied_cents`, `net_balance_cents` (no shape change) | The unapplied payments list read through no branch wall, so a caller pinned to one yard saw another yard's payment number, day and amount. | The list follows the caller's reach, as the open documents do. `unapplied_cents` and `net_balance_cents` stay whole account behind the customer wall, as the closing balance does (the balance shows at every yard, the documents behind it follow grants), so a pinned caller's listed rows may sum to less than `unapplied_cents`; the page says so in one line. One exception stays: a legacy counter return credit (`source_kind` `pos_return`, written by code with no caller left) is a subledger row with no document, so the statement shows it and the aging does not; it touches rows already in a database only, and the net balance equals the aging for every row the code writes today. |
| G1-QTY (item 130, note N2) | pos: `PATCH /api/v1/pos/transactions/{id}/items/{itemId}` (new route, no migration) | A line's quantity could not change once it was on the sale: only the add and the remove existed, so a cashier removed the line and added it again. | Body `{"quantity", "revision"}` (or `If-Match`). The line is priced again at the new quantity by the step the add uses (quantity breaks, kit components, the sale's tax), keeps its id and its place, keeps a price the cashier overrode and a discount, and the sale's subtotal, tax and total are summed as every line write sums them, so the result equals removing the line and adding it at the new quantity. A stale revision is 409 `stale_revision` and none is 428; a sale that is not open is 409 `invalid_state_transition`; a quantity of zero or less, a text line or a kit component is 400; a line not on this sale is 404. Roles, the branch wall and the no header rule are the add's; the bound key class is WALLED. No golden changes. |

The image route is unchanged (generation runs after it answers; a refusal there is the media row's failed status). The characterization harness points the server's provider at a local stand-in so a save's test never reaches the network. |

A stored test result from an older settings revision reads `untested`. A save that cannot reach the provider keeps the key and reads `failed` with "The key is saved. <Provider> could not be reached to test it; press Test connection later."; its test has a timeout of its own. The sanitiser drops a model's `text` and `tspan` elements. |

Round 2: a reorder line is found on a draft by vendor, yard and source (REORDER or the person's MANUAL draft; never a special order's), and a product with no reorder quantity orders its deficit, with none at its point, and the run's record names it (`reorder_runs` error_message, products_skipped). Purchasing recommendations (`GET /api/v1/purchase-orders/recommendations`) read per yard: rows gain `branch_id`, `branch_name` (additive, required), one row per product and yard from that yard's stock and sales, and an optional `vendor_id` (additive, absent when the product has no vendor) so a new purchase order from the row is created for that vendor and at that yard. Goldens moved: `product_item` (rows per yard, 52 rows across the three yards), `purchase_order_flow` (the reorder check creates 4 drafts, REORDER 1 to 4; the recommendations step's body is masked: the per yard rows over the seed vary per run and are pinned by legs). For a live dealer: before this change reorder drafts sat at one resolved branch carrying every yard's lines; the first run after the upgrade writes per yard drafts and does not reuse those old combined drafts, so the same products can be on order twice until someone deletes the old draft. |

The Today board's money is integer cents. Scheduling: the dealer defines per yard weekly delivery windows and pickup slots (weekday, start, end, capacity, cutoff; local hours and minutes in the yard's zone, instants UTC), walled to the caller's yards; the contractor lists the concrete slots with the places left and holds, changes or releases one, capacity enforced in a transaction that locks the window row (a full window is 409 `SCHEDULE_WINDOW_FULL`, past the cutoff 409 `SCHEDULE_CUTOFF_PASSED`), an optional own unfulfilled order riding the hold (stamping `orders.scheduled_delivery_date` and any open delivery's `scheduled_start` and `scheduled_end`). | M29 (CP-05, IX-01) | pos: `GET /api/v1/pos/terminal-intents/{id}`, `POST /api/v1/pos/terminal-intents/{id}/cancel`, `POST /api/v1/pos/terminal-intents/sweep`, `GET /api/v1/pos/terminal-lost-items`, `POST /api/v1/pos/terminal-lost-items/{id}/resolve` (new routes); migration 128 | A card leg at the counter had no record of its own: a terminal that never answered left the sale, the drawer and the money to guesswork, and a lost answer had no queue or resolution | A card leg is claimed and committed before the device is called, its dispatch move to `pending_at_device` is committed before the call too, and every state move is one guarded statement. The sweep (a manager's trigger, sweeping only the caller's reach; the worker's own pass sweeps every branch) turns a lapsed leg lost in one statement with its manager item. The five routes carry the branch wall: a header caller outside the branch finds a missing row (404), a caller with no header and no grant is 403, and the queue lists only the caller's branches. The resolve route claims the item first (409 `lost_item_settled` for a second or racing resolve) and settles the money with today's paths: an authorized leg on an open sale stays for its completion (409 `authorized_leg_unused` for a completion that would leave it behind), on a completed sale it applies up to the invoice's open amount and the rest is put back, on a sale paid in full or voided the whole charge is put back (502 `charge_not_reversed` and the item open again when the reversal fails), and a stranded void, return or payback leg settles through its refund claim's own resolution; a declined resolution closes the item (or moves an unspent authorized leg to declined; 409 `terminal_leg_already_booked` when the leg is already booked on the sale). The lost item carries the device's late verdict and reference (`late_verdict`, `late_provider_ref`) and a contradicting late answer (authorized after resolved declined, or declined after resolved authorized) reopens the item. Completing a sale with a card tender whose terminal leg is claimed or at the device is 409 `terminal_leg_in_flight`, whose answer is lost and unresolved 409 `terminal_answer_unresolved`, and a declined or closed leg retries as a new attempt |
